\rm b_{4}
).
∎

4.
\tau_{\mathcal{C}}
-lower semicontinuity

In this section we prove Theorem
2.5
by following the arguments of
[
36
, Proposition 4.1]
, and in particular by using density estimates for some Radon measures associated to
\mathcal{F}.
We start with the following lower bound for the localized surface energy.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1),

Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\subset\subset\mathrm{Int}(\Omega\cup\Sigma\cup S),

r>0,

\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1},
be a cube and
\Gamma\subset Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})
be an
(n-1)
-dimensional Lipschitz graph separating
Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})
into two connected components such that

(a1)

x_{0}\in\Gamma,

\nu_{\Gamma}(x_{0})=\nu
and

|\nu_{\Gamma}(x)-\nu|<\delta\quad\text{and}\quad|(x-x_{0})\cdot\nu|<\tfrac{\delta r}{2}\quad\text{for all $x\in\Gamma;$}

(a2)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\Gamma)<(1+\delta)r^{n-1}.

Assume that a sequence
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}\subset\mathcal{C}
and a configuration
(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}
satisfy

(a3)

u_{k}=\xi
for some
\xi\in(0,1)^{n}\setminus\Xi_{\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}}
(see Remark
2.1
) and

M:=\sup_{k\geq 1}\mathcal{F}(A_{k},u_{k})<+\infty;

(a4)

A_{k}\to A
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n});

(a5)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\partial^{*}(A\cup S))<\delta r^{n-1}
and
|(A\cup S)\cap Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})|>(1-\delta)r^{n};

(a6)

either

u_{k}\to u\quad\text{a.e.\ in $Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})$}

and

K:=Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap J_{u}

or there exists a set of finite perimeter
E\subset Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})
such that

u_{k}\to u\quad\text{a.e.\ in $Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\setminus E$}\qquad\text{and}\qquad|u_{k}|\to+\infty\quad\text{a.e.\ in $Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap E,$}

and

K:=Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\partial^{*}E

(see Figure
2
).

Figure 2
.
Set
K
in Proposition
4.1
.

(a7)

the set
K
satisfies

(a7.1)

\nu_{K}(x_{0})=\nu
and
\theta(K,x_{0})=\theta(\Gamma\cap K,x_{0})=1;

(a7.2)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(K\cap\Gamma)>(1-\delta)r^{n-1};

(a7.3)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(K\setminus\Gamma)<\delta r^{n-1}.

We also denote by
\phi
a norm in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying

(4.1)

b_{1}\leq\phi(\nu)\leq b_{2},\quad\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}.

Let
C,D\subset\subset Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})
be given by Lemma
3.1
applied with
\delta,

\Gamma
and
K.
Then there exist
c^{\prime}=c_{b_{2}}^{\prime}>0
and
k_{\delta}^{\prime}:=k_{\delta}^{\prime}(b_{2})>0
such that for any
k>k_{\delta}^{\prime}:

(i)

if
Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\subset\subset\Omega,
then

\displaystyle\int_{D\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{D\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}

\displaystyle\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\int_{K}\phi(\nu_{K})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime}\delta r^{n-1}

(4.2)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial D}\phi(\nu_{D})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-(c^{\prime}+5b_{2})\delta r^{n-1};

(ii)

if
x_{0}\in\Sigma
and
\Gamma=Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\Sigma,
then

\displaystyle\int_{C\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{C\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{\Sigma\cap\partial C\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\int_{K}\phi(\nu_{K})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime}\delta r^{n-1}

(4.3)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial C}\phi(\nu_{C})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-(c^{\prime}+5b_{2})\delta r^{n-1}.

The proof of this proposition is left after the proof of Theorem
2.5
. In the proof of lower semicontinuity we only use the following corollary of Proposition
4.1
; the assertions including sets
C,

D
and
E
will used in the proof of compactness.

Corollary 4.2
.

Under assumptions of Proposition
4.1
, together with

(a6)

u_{k}\to u\quad\text{a.e.\ in $Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})$}

and

K:=Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap J_{u},

there exist
c^{\prime}=c_{b_{2}}^{\prime}>0
and
k_{\delta}^{\prime}:=k_{\delta}^{\prime}(b_{2})>0
such that for any
k>k_{\delta}^{\prime}:

(i)

if
Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\subset\subset\Omega,
then

\int_{Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\\
\geq 2\int_{K}\phi(\nu_{K})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime}\delta r^{n-1};

(ii)

if
x_{0}\in\Sigma
and
\Gamma=Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\Sigma,
then

\int_{Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\\
+2\int_{Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\geq 2\int_{K}\phi(\nu_{K})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime}\delta r^{n-1}.

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

In view of Remark
2.1
we may assume that
u_{k}=\xi
for some
\xi\in(0,1)^{n}\setminus\Xi_{\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}}.

Moreover, there is no loss of generality in assuming
liminf
in (
2.14
) is a finite limit. Thus,

M:=\sup\limits_{k\geq 1}\,\mathcal{F}(A_{k},u_{k})<+\infty.

In particular,
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}
satisfies the assumptions (a3) and (a4) of Proposition
4.1
.

Let

\displaystyle\mu_{k}(B):=

\displaystyle\int_{B\cap\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\varphi(x,\nu_{A_{k}})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{B\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+\int_{B\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\setminus J_{u_{k}}}[\beta+\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})]\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{B\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+\int_{B\cap\Sigma\setminus\partial^{*}A_{k}}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+\int_{B\cap(A\cup S)}W(x,\mathcal{E}u_{k}-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}x

and

\displaystyle\mu(B):=

\displaystyle\int_{B\cap\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A}\varphi(x,\nu_{A})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{B\cap A^{(1)}\cap J_{u}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{u}})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+\int_{B\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A\setminus J_{u}}[\beta+\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})]\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{B\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A\cap J_{u}}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+\int_{B\cap\Sigma\setminus\partial^{*}A}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+\int_{B\cap(A\cup S)}W(x,\mathcal{E}u-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}x

be positive Radon measures in
\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Notice that

(4.4)

\mu_{k}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=\mathcal{F}(A_{k},u_{k})+\int_{\Sigma}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

and

(4.5)

\mu(\mathbb{R}^{n})=\mathcal{F}(A,u)+\int_{\Sigma}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

In particular,

\sup\limits_{k\geq 1}\mu_{k}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\leq M+\int_{\Sigma}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1},

and thus, there exist a positive Radon measure
\mu_{0}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and a not relabelled subsequence
\{\mu_{k}\}
such that
\mu_{k}\rightharpoonup^{*}\mu_{0}.

Let us show

(4.6)

\mu_{0}\geq\mu.

Note that (
2.14
) directly follows from (
4.6
), (
4.4
), (
4.5
). By the nonnegativity of
\mu
and
\mu_{0},
and the explicit form of the support of
\mu,
to establish (
4.6
) it suffices to prove the following density estimates:

(4.7a)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits[\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A]}\,(x)\geq\varphi(x,\nu_{A}(x))\quad\text{$\mathcal{H}^{n-1}$-a.e.\ $x\in(\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A)\cup(\Sigma\setminus\partial^{*}A),$}

(4.7b)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits[A^{(1)}\cap J_{u}]}\,(x)\geq 2\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{u}}(x))\quad\text{$\mathcal{H}^{n-1}$-a.e.\ $x\in A^{(1)}\cap J_{u},$}

(4.7c)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits[\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A\cap J_{u}]}\,(x)\geq 2\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma}(x))\quad\text{$\mathcal{H}^{n-1}$-a.e.\ $x\in\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A\cap J_{u},$}

(4.7d)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits[\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A]}\,(x)\geq\beta(x)+\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma}(x))\quad\text{$\mathcal{H}^{n-1}$-a.e.\ $x\in\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A,$}

(4.7e)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits[\Sigma\setminus\partial^{*}A]}\,(x)\geq\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma}(x))\quad\text{$\mathcal{H}^{n-1}$-a.e.\ $x\in\Sigma\setminus\partial^{*}A,$}

(4.7f)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}^{n}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits[A\cup S]}\,(x)\geq W(x,\mathcal{E}u(x)-\mathbf{M}_{0}(x))\quad\text{$\mathcal{L}^{n}$-a.e.\ $x\in A\cup S.$}

Proofs of (
4.7a
), (
4.7d
) and (
4.7e
).

By assumptions (H1)-(H3), the capillary functional

\mathcal{C}(E;U)=\int_{U\cap\partial^{*}E}\varphi(x,\nu_{E})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+\int_{U\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}E}[\beta+\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})]\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+\int_{U\cap\Sigma\setminus\partial^{*}E}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

is
L^{1}(U)
-lowersemicontinuous in any open set
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
(see e.g.,
[
1
, Theorem 3.4]
). As
A_{k}\to A
and
\mu_{k}\rightharpoonup^{*}\mu_{0},
for any ball
B_{r}(x_{0})
with
\mu_{0}(\partial B_{r}(x_{0}))=0
we have

\displaystyle\mu_{0}(B_{r}(x_{0}))=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to+\infty}\mu_{k}(B_{r}(x_{0}))\geq\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{C}(A_{k},B_{r}(x_{0}))\geq\mathcal{C}(A,B_{r}(x_{0})).

This inequality and the Besicovitch differentiation theorem imply (
4.7a
), (
4.7d
) and (
4.7e
).

Proof of (
4.7b
).
Fix
\epsilon\in(0,2^{-10})
and let
K:=A^{(1)}\cap J_{u}.
By the
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-rectifiability of
K
, there exists an at most countable family
\{\Gamma_{l}\}
of
(n-1)
-dimensional
C^{1}
-graphs such that

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(K\setminus\bigcup_{l\geq 1}\Gamma_{l}\Big)=0.

Let
x_{0}\in L
be such that

(
\rm a_{1}
)

x_{0}\in\Gamma_{l}
for some
l\geq 1
so that the generalized unit normal
\nu_{0}:=\nu_{K}(x_{0})
to
L
at
x_{0}
exists and equals to
\nu_{\Gamma_{l}}(x_{0})
;

(
\rm a_{2}
)

\theta(K,x_{0})=\theta(\Gamma_{l}\cap K,x_{0})=1;

(
\rm a_{3}
)

\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits K}(x_{0})
exists;

(
\rm a_{4}
)

\lim\limits_{r\to 0}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\int_{Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap K}\varphi(x_{0},\nu_{K})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=\varphi(x_{0},\nu_{0}).

By the
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-rectifiability of
K,

[
3
, Theorem 2.63]
and Lebesgue-Besicovitch differentiation theorem, the set of
x_{0}\in K
for which at least one of these conditions fails is
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-negligible.
Since
\varphi
is uniformly continuous in
\overline{\Omega},
there exists
r_{1,\epsilon}>0
such that

(4.8)

|\varphi(x,\nu)-\varphi(y,\nu)|<\epsilon\quad\text{whenever $|x-y|<r_{1,\epsilon}$ and $\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}.$}

Decreasing
r_{1,\epsilon}
if necessary, we assume that
Q_{r_{1,\epsilon},\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\subset\subset\Omega.
Then for any
r\in(0,r_{1,\epsilon})

(4.9)

\mu_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))\geq\alpha_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))\\
-\epsilon\Big(\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\partial^{*}A_{k})+2\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}})\Big),

where

\alpha_{k}(U):=\int_{U\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{U\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

and
\phi(\nu):=\varphi(x_{0},\nu).
By assumption (
2.8
) and the nonnegativity of the summands of
\mu_{k}
we have an a priori bound

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\partial^{*}A_{k})+2\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}})\leq\frac{\mu(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))}{b_{1}},

and thus, inserting this in (
4.9
) we get

(4.10)

\Big(1+\frac{\epsilon}{b_{1}}\Big)\mu_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))\geq\alpha_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})).

Now we estimate
\alpha_{k}
from below using Corollary
4.2
(a). Since
\Gamma_{l}
is a
C^{1}
-graph, by (
a_{1}
) there exists
r_{2,\epsilon}\in(0,r_{1,\epsilon})
such that

•

\Gamma_{l}
divides the cube
Q_{r_{2,\epsilon},\nu_{0}}(x_{0})
into two connected components;

•

|\nu_{\Gamma_{l}}(x)-\nu_{0}|<\epsilon
for any
x\in Q_{r_{2,\epsilon},\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\Gamma_{l};

•

|(x-x_{0})\cdot\nu_{0}|<\epsilon r/2
for any
r\in(0,r_{2,\epsilon})
and
x\in Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\Gamma_{l};

•

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\Gamma_{l})<(1+\epsilon)r^{n-1}
for all
r\in(0,r_{2,\epsilon}).

In particular, for any
r\in(0,r_{2,\epsilon})
the cube
Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})
and the
C^{1}
-graph
\Gamma:=Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\Gamma_{l}
satisfy the assumptions (a1)-(a2) of Proposition
4.1
. As we mentioned in the beginning of the proof,
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}
satisfies (a3)-(a4) of Proposition
4.1
.
Moreover, by assumptions
x_{0}\in A^{(1)}
and (
a_{2}
) there exists
r_{3,\epsilon}\in(0,r_{2,\epsilon})
such that

•

P(A,Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))<\epsilon r^{n-1}
and
|A\cap Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})|>(1-\epsilon)r^{n-1}
for all
r\in(0,r_{3,\epsilon});

•

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap K\cap\Gamma_{l})>(1-\epsilon)r^{n-1}
for any
r\in(0,r_{3,\epsilon});

•

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap K\setminus\Gamma_{l})<\delta r^{n-1}
for any
r\in(0,r_{3,\epsilon}).

Thus, assumptions (a5)-(a7) of Proposition
4.1
also hold. Therefore, by Corollary
4.2
(i) there exists
k_{\epsilon}>0
and
c^{\prime}>0
such that

\alpha_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))\geq 2\int_{Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap K}\phi(\nu_{K})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime}\epsilon r^{n-1}

for all
k>k_{\epsilon}.

This and (
4.10
) yield

\Big(1+\frac{\epsilon}{b_{1}}\Big)\mu_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))\geq 2\int_{Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap K}\phi(\nu_{K})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime}\epsilon r^{n-1}.

Now letting
k\to+\infty
for a.e.
r\in(0,r_{3,\epsilon})
we get

\Big(1+\frac{\epsilon}{b_{1}}\Big)\mu_{0}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))\geq 2\int_{Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap K}\phi(\nu_{K})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime}\epsilon r^{n-1}.

Therefore, by (
a_{3}
) and (
a_{4}
)

\Big(1+\frac{\epsilon}{b_{1}}\Big)\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits K}(x_{0})=\Big(1+\frac{\epsilon}{b_{1}}\Big)\lim\limits_{r\to 0^{+}}\frac{\mu_{0}(Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0}))}{r^{n-1}}\geq 2\varphi(x_{0},\nu_{0})-c^{\prime}\epsilon.

Now letting
\epsilon\to 0
we obtain (
4.7b
).

Proof of (
4.7c
).
Let
\epsilon\in(0,2^{-10})
and let
L:=\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A\cap J_{u}.
Since
\Sigma
is Lipschitz,
L
is
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-rectifiable.

Let
x_{0}\in L
be such that

(
\rm b_{1}
)

\nu_{0}:=\nu_{\Sigma}(x_{0})
exist and equals to
\nu_{L}(x_{0});

(
\rm b_{2}
)

\theta(L,x_{0})=\theta(\Sigma,x_{0})=\theta(\partial^{*}A,x_{0})=1;

(
\rm b_{3}
)

\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits L}(x_{0})
exists.

(
\rm b_{4}
)

\lim\limits_{r\to 0}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\int_{Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap L}\varphi(x_{0},\nu_{J_{u}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=\varphi(x_{0},\nu_{0}).

By the lipschitzianity of
\Sigma,

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-rectifiablity of
\partial^{*}A,

[
3
, Theorem 2.63]
and Besicovitch differentiation theorem, the set of
x_{0}\in L
for which at least one of these conditions fails is
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-negligible.

Let
r_{1,\epsilon}>0
be such that (
4.8
) holds and
Q_{r_{1,\epsilon},\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\subset\subset{\rm Int}{(\Omega\cup S\cup\Sigma)}.
Then as in (
4.10
)

\displaystyle\Big(1+\frac{\epsilon}{b_{1}}\Big)\mu_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))\geq\gamma_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))

for any
r\in(0,r_{1,\epsilon}),
where

\gamma_{k}(U):=\int_{U\cap\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{U\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{U\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{\Sigma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

Since
\Sigma
is Lipschitz continuous, by (
\rm b_{1}
) and (
\rm b_{2}
) there exists
r_{2,\epsilon}\in(0,r_{1,\epsilon})
such that

•

\Sigma
divides the cube
Q_{r_{2,\epsilon},\nu_{0}}(x_{0})
into two connected components;

•

|\nu_{\Sigma}(x)-\nu_{\Sigma}(x_{0})|<\epsilon
for any
x\in Q_{r_{2,\epsilon},\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\Sigma;

•

|(x-x_{0})\cdot\nu_{0}|<\epsilon r/2
for any
r\in(0,r_{2,\epsilon})
and
x\in Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\Sigma;

•

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\Sigma)<(1+\epsilon)r^{n-1}
for all
r\in(0,r_{2,\epsilon}).

Moreover, since
x_{0}\in\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A
and
\theta(L,x_{0})=\theta(\partial^{*}A,x_{0})=1,
there exists
r_{3,\epsilon}\in(0,r_{2,\epsilon})
such that

•

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A)>(1-\epsilon)r^{n-1}
and
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\partial^{*}A\setminus\Sigma)<\delta r^{n-1}.

Thus, applying Corollary
4.2
(b) we find
k_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}>0
and
c^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

\gamma_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))\geq 2\int_{Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap L}\phi(\nu_{\Sigma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime\prime}\delta r^{n-1}

for all
k>k_{\epsilon}^{\prime\prime}.
Therefore,

\Big(1+\frac{\epsilon}{b_{1}}\Big)\mu_{k}(Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0}))\geq 2\int_{Q_{r,\nu_{0}}(x_{0})\cap L}\phi(\nu_{\Sigma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime\prime}\delta r^{n-1}

and hence, by (
\rm b_{3}
) and (
\rm b_{4}
)

\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\mathop{\hbox{\vrule height=9.0pt,width=0.5pt,depth=0.0pt\vrule height=0.5pt,width=5.0pt,depth=0.0pt}}\nolimits L}(x_{0})\geq 2\varphi(x_{0},\nu_{0}).

Proof of (
4.7f
).
By the nonnegativity of
\mu_{k}
and our assumption
u_{k}=\xi
on
\Omega\setminus A_{k}

\displaystyle\mu_{k}(B_{r}(x))\geq

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}(x)\cap(A_{k}\cup S)}W(y,\mathcal{E}u_{k}-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}y

(4.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}(x)\cap(\Omega\cup S)}W(y,\mathcal{E}u_{k}-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}y-\int_{B_{r}(x)\cap(\Omega\setminus A_{k})}W(y,-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}y.

Since
\mu_{k}\rightharpoonup^{*}\mu_{0},

\mathcal{E}u_{k}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{E}u
in
L^{2}(\Omega\cup S)
(see (
2.11
)) and
A_{k}\to A
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),
letting
k\to+\infty
in (
4
) for any ball
B_{r}(x)
with
\mu_{0}(\partial B_{r}(x))=0,
we get

\displaystyle\mu_{0}(B_{r}(x))=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\to+\infty}\mu_{k}(B_{r}(x))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}(x)\cap(\Omega\cup S)}W(y,\mathcal{E}u-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}y-\int_{B_{r}(x)\cap(\Omega\setminus A)}W(y,-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}(x)\cap(A\cup S)}W(y,\mathcal{E}u-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}y,

where in the equality we used
u=\xi
in
\Omega\setminus A.
Now (
4.7f
) follows from the Besicovitch differentiation theorem.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

According to the proof of Theorem
2.5
both
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{W}
are
\tau_{\mathcal{C}}
-lower semicontinuous in
\mathcal{C}.

Now we prove bounds (
(i)
)-(
(ii)
).

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

We only prove (i).
The last inequality in (
(i)
) directly follows from (
3.1
)-(
3.3
). Therefore, we establish only the first estimate.
Without loss of generality, we assume
x_{0}=0,

r=1
and
\nu={\bf e}_{n}.
By (a1)

\Gamma\subset(-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2})^{n-1}\times(-\frac{\delta}{2},\frac{\delta}{2}),

by (a3) and a priori estimates in Remark
2.3

(4.12)

M_{1}:=\sup\limits_{k\geq 1}\Big(\int_{\Omega\cup S}|\mathcal{E}u_{k}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{k}})\Big)<+\infty.

We prove (
(i)
) for
K=Q_{1}\cap\partial^{*}E
(i.e., in the case
|u_{k}|\to+\infty
a.e. in
Q_{1}\cap E
); the other case being similar. For any open set
G\subset Q_{1}
define

\alpha_{k}(G):=\int_{G\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{G\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

Step 1.
Let

\Upsilon:=\{\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}:\,\,|\xi\cdot e_{n}|\geq 2\delta\}.

Then by (a1) for any
\xi\in\Upsilon
and
x\in Q_{1}\cap\Gamma

|\xi\cdot\nu_{\Gamma}(x)|\geq|\xi\cdot{\bf e}_{n}|-|\xi\cdot(\nu_{\Gamma}(x)-{\bf e}_{n})|>\delta,

and hence,
Q_{1}\cap\Gamma
is a graph also in
\xi
-direction, i.e., for any
y\in\Pi_{\xi}
the line
\pi_{\xi}^{-1}(y)
intersects
Q_{1}\cap\Gamma
at most at one point.

Step 2.
Let
D
be given by Lemma
3.1
and let
U\subset\subset D
be any open set such that
U\cap\Gamma\cap K\neq\emptyset.

Let also
(B_{k}^{U},v_{k}^{U})
be given by Corollary
3.3
applied with
U,

\delta=\frac{|U|}{k}
and
(A_{k},u_{k})
.
Then for all
k:

(
\rm a_{1}
)

B_{k}^{U}\subset A_{k},

A_{k}\setminus B_{k}^{U}\subset\subset U
and
|A_{k}\setminus B_{k}^{U}|<1/k;

(
\rm a_{2}
)

v_{k}^{U}=u_{k}
in
B_{k}^{U}\cup S;

(
\rm a_{3}
)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[B_{k}^{U}]^{(1)}\cap J_{v_{k}^{U}})<1/k;

(
a_{4}
)

\alpha_{k}(U)+|U|/k\geq\Lambda_{k}(U),
where

\Lambda_{k}(U):=\int_{U\cap\partial^{*}B_{k}^{U}}\phi(\nu_{B_{k}^{U}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

By (
\rm a_{1}
)
B_{k}^{U}\to A
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and by (
\rm a_{1}
), (
\rm a_{2}
) and also (a6)

(4.13)

v_{k}^{U}\to u\,\,\,\,\text{a.e.\ in $U\setminus E$}\qquad\text{and}\qquad|v_{k}^{U}|\to+\infty\,\,\,\,\text{a.e.\ in $U\cap E.$}

Moreover, by (
4.12
) and (
\rm a_{2}
)

\sup_{k\geq 1}\Big(\int_{U}|\mathcal{E}v_{k}^{U}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap J_{v_{k}^{U}})\Big)<+\infty.

We claim that

(4.14)

\displaystyle\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\,\Lambda_{k}(U)\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\phi^{o}(\xi)}\,\int_{U\cap\Gamma}|\nu_{\Gamma}\cdot\xi|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-2b_{2}P(A,U)-2b_{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E]).

for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
\xi\in\Upsilon.

To prove (
4.14
) we study some properties of one-dimensional slices
[\widehat{v}_{k}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}
of
v_{k}^{U}.
We closely follow the arguments of
[
14
, pp. 11-13]
; see also
[
15
]
. Let
k_{j}:=k_{j}^{U}
be such that

\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\int_{U\cap J_{v_{k}^{U}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{v_{k}^{U}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=\lim\limits_{j\to+\infty}\int_{U\cap J_{v_{k_{j}}^{U}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{v_{k_{j}}^{U}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

Applying (
2.3
) and (
2.4
) with
v=v_{k_{j}}^{U},
(
2.5
) with
L=J_{v_{k_{j}}^{U}},
and using (
4.12
)
we find

(4.15)

\liminf\limits_{j\to+\infty}\int_{\Pi_{\xi}}\Big[\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}})+\kappa I_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}}^{U})+\kappa II_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}}^{U})\Big]\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)<+\infty

for any
\kappa>0
and
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
\xi\in\Upsilon.

Moreover, by
[
14
, Lemma 2.7]
and (
4.13
)

(4.16)

|v_{k}^{U}\cdot\xi|\to+\infty\quad\text{a.e.\ in $U\cap E$}

for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
\xi\in\Upsilon.

Fix any
\xi\in\Upsilon
satisfying (
4.15
) and (
4.16
) and consider the one-dimensional slices
[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}
and
\widehat{u}_{y}^{\xi}.

In view of (
4.15
) and Fatou’s lemma, for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
y\in\pi_{\xi}(U)

\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\Big[\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}})+\kappa I_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}}^{U})+\kappa II_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}}^{U})\Big]<+\infty.

Thus, for
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.
y\in\pi_{\xi}(U)
there exists a subsequence
\{k_{j}^{y}\}\subset\{k_{j}\}
(depending also on
\kappa>0
) such that

(4.17)

\liminf\limits_{j\to+\infty}\Big[\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}})+\kappa I_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}}^{U})+\kappa II_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}}^{U})\Big]\\
=\lim\limits_{j\to+\infty}\Big[\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}})+\kappa I_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U})+\kappa II_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U})\Big],

and by (
4.13
) and (
4.16
)

(4.18)

[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}\to\widehat{u}_{y}^{\xi}\quad\text{$\mathcal{L}^{1}$-a.e.\ in $[U\setminus E]_{y}^{\xi}$}\quad\text{and}\quad\Big|[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}\Big|\to+\infty\quad\text{$\mathcal{L}^{1}$-a.e.\ in $[U\cap E]_{y}^{\xi}.$}

For
\tau(t)=\arctan(t),
set
f_{j}:=\tau\circ[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}.
Then
f_{j}\in SBV_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(U_{y}^{\xi})
and
J_{[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}}=J_{f_{j}}.
By (
4.17
), (
4.18
) and
[
2
, Proposition 4.2]
we find a not relabelled subsequence
\{v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}\}
such that

f_{j}\to f_{0}\quad\text{$\mathcal{L}^{1}$-a.e.\ in $U_{y}^{\xi}$ as $j\to+\infty.$}

By (
4.18
)

\begin{cases}f_{0}=\tau\circ\widehat{u}_{y}^{U}&\text{in $[U\setminus E]_{y}^{\xi},$}\\
|f_{0}|=\pi/2&\text{in $[U\cap E]_{y}^{\xi}.$}\end{cases}

By
[
2
, Proposition 4.2]

(4.19)

\liminf\limits_{j\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{0}\Big(J_{[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}}\Big)=\liminf\limits_{j\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{f_{j}})\geq\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{f_{0}}).

Thus,
\mathcal{H}^{0}(U_{y}^{\xi}\cap J_{f_{0}})<+\infty
and hence,
[U\cap E]_{y}^{\xi}
consists of finitely many segments in each of which either
f_{0}\equiv\pi/2
or
f_{0}\equiv-\pi/2.

By (
4.17
)

\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{f_{j}})
is uniformly bounded and hence, there exists a further not relabelled subsequence and
N_{y}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that

\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{f_{j}})=N_{y}\quad\text{and}\quad J_{f_{j}}=\{t_{j}^{1},\ldots,t_{j}^{N_{y}}\}\subset U_{y}^{\xi}\quad\text{for all $j.$}

Then points of
J_{f_{j}}
converges to
M_{y}\leq N_{y}
points
t^{1}<\ldots<t^{M_{y}}.

Since
II_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U})
is uniformly bounded, the precise representatives of
f_{j}
uniformly bounded in
W_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1,1}(t^{l},t^{l+1})
so that
f_{j}\to f_{0}
locally uniformly in
(t^{l},t^{l+1})
and
J_{f_{0}}\subset\{t^{1},\ldots,t^{M_{y}}\}.
Repeating the arguments of
[
14
, Section 1]
we can show that
t^{1}:=U_{y}^{\xi}\cap[\partial^{*}E]_{y}^{\xi}\in J_{f_{0}}.

Let us estimate the
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-measures of the sets

\displaystyle Y_{0}:=

\displaystyle\{y\in\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U\cap K):\,\,N_{y}=0\},

\displaystyle Y_{1}:=

\displaystyle\{y\in\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U\cap K):\,\,N_{y}=1\},

\displaystyle Y_{2}:=

\displaystyle\{y\in\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U\cap K):\,\,N_{y}\geq 2\}.

By (
4.19
)
\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{f_{0}})=0
for any
y\in Y_{0}.
Hence,
U\cap\pi_{\xi}^{-1}(y)\cap(\partial^{*}E\cup J_{u})=\emptyset,
and therefore

Y_{0}\subset\pi_{\xi}(U\cap\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E).

Then by the
1
-Lipschitz continuity of the projection
\pi_{\xi}

(4.20)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Y_{0})\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\pi_{\xi}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E]))\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E]).

Now consider any
y\in Y_{1}.
By definition
\pi_{\xi}^{-1}(y)
intersects
U\cap J_{v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}}
just once and therefore, by the construction of
(B_{k}^{U},v_{k}^{U})
(see the proof of Corollary
3.3
) either

y\in\pi_{\xi}\Big(U\cap[B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k_{j}^{y}}}\cap J_{v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}}\Big)

or

y\in\pi_{\xi}(U\setminus B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}).

If
y\in\pi_{\xi}(U\setminus B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}),
then
t_{j}^{1}
divides the line
U\cap\pi_{\xi}^{-1}(y)
into two parts one is a subset of
U\cap B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}
and the other is that of
U\setminus B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}.
Since
B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}\to A
and
t^{1}=U_{y}^{\xi}\cap[\partial^{*}E]_{y}^{\xi}\in J_{f_{0}},
it follows that
t^{1}\in\partial^{*}A
and divides
U\cap\pi_{\xi}^{-1}(y)
into two parts one belonging to
U\cap A
other to
U\setminus A.
In particular,
y\in\pi_{\xi}(U\cap\partial^{*}A).

Hence,

y\in\Big[U\cap[B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k_{j}^{y}}}\cap J_{v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}})\Big]_{y}^{\xi}\cup\Big[U\cap\partial^{*}A\Big]_{y}^{\xi}

for all
j.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Y_{1})=

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{1}}\mathcal{H}^{0}\left(\bigcap_{j}\Big(\Big[U\cap[B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k_{j}^{y}}}\cap J_{v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}})\Big]_{y}^{\xi}\cup\Big[U\setminus B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}\Big]_{y}^{\xi}\Big)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{1}}\lim\limits_{j\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{0}\Big(\Big[U\cap[B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k_{j}^{y}}}\cap J_{v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}})\Big]_{y}^{\xi}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)

\displaystyle+\int_{Y_{1}}\mathcal{H}^{0}\Big(\Big[U\cap\partial^{*}A\Big]_{y}^{\xi}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y).

By the choice of
\{k_{j}^{y}\}
, the Fatou’s lemma, the second equality in (
2.5
) and (
\rm a_{3}
)

\int_{Y_{1}}\lim\limits_{j\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{0}\Big(\Big[U\cap[B_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k_{j}^{y}}}\cap J_{v_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}})\Big]_{y}^{\xi}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)\\
\leq\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(U\cap[B_{k}^{U}]^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}\cap J_{v_{k}^{U}})\Big)=0.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{1}}\mathcal{H}^{0}\Big(\Big[U\cap\partial^{*}A\Big]_{y}^{\xi}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)\leq

\displaystyle P(A,U).

Thus,

(4.21)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Y_{1})\leq P(A,U).

Now using
\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U)=Y_{0}\cup Y_{1}\cup Y_{2},
from (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) we obtain

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}([\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U)]\setminus Y_{2})\leq P(A,U)+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E]).

Moreover, let

X:=\{y\in\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U):\,\,\text{$\pi_{\xi}^{-1}(y)\cap\Gamma\cap\partial^{*}E$ is a singleton}\}.

Then as above

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Y_{2}\setminus X)\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}([\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U)]\setminus X)\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}([U\cap(\Gamma\cup\partial^{*}A)]\setminus\partial^{*}E)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E])+P(A,U),

and therefore,

(4.22)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}([\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U)]\setminus[Y_{2}\cap X])\leq 2P(A,U)+2\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E]).

By the definition of
X
and
Y_{2}
for any
y\in Y_{2}\cap X
we have
\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{f_{0}})=1
and
N_{y}\geq 2,
therefore,
we can improve (
4.19
) as

\lim\limits_{j\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{0}\Big(J_{[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}^{y}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}}\Big)\geq 2=2\mathcal{H}^{0}([U\cap\Gamma]_{y}^{\xi}).

For such
y
from (
4.17
) we get

\liminf\limits_{j\to+\infty}\Big[\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{[\widehat{v}_{k_{j}}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}})+\kappa I_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}}^{U})+\kappa II_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k_{j}}^{U})\Big]\geq 2\mathcal{H}^{0}([U\cap\Gamma]_{y}^{\xi})

Now integrating over
X\cap Y_{2}
and using (
4.15
) and the Fatou’s lemma we get

\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\int_{\Pi_{\xi}}\Big[\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{[\widehat{v}_{k}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}})+\kappa I_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k}^{U})+\kappa II_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k}^{U})\Big]\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)\geq 2\int_{X\cap Y_{2}}\mathcal{H}^{0}([U\cap\Gamma]_{y}^{\xi})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y).

By the definition of
\Upsilon,

\mathcal{H}^{0}([U\cap\Gamma]_{y}^{\xi})=1
for all
y\in\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U)
and therefore by (
4.22
)

\int_{X\cap Y_{2}}\mathcal{H}^{0}([U\cap\Gamma]_{y}^{\xi})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)\geq\int_{\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U)}\mathcal{H}^{0}([U\cap\Gamma]_{y}^{\xi})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)\\
-P(A,U)-\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E]).

Hence,

\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\int_{\Pi_{\xi}}\Big[\mathcal{H}^{0}(J_{[\widehat{v}_{k}^{U}]_{y}^{\xi}})+\kappa I_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k}^{U})+\kappa II_{y,\xi}^{U}(v_{k}^{U})\Big]\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)\\
\geq 2\int_{\Pi_{\xi}\cap\pi_{\xi}(U)}\mathcal{H}^{0}([U\cap\Gamma]_{y}^{\xi})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)-2P(A,U)-2\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E]).

This, (
2.5
), (
2.3
), (
2.4
) as well as (
4.12
) yield

(4.23)

\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\int_{U\cap J_{v_{k}^{U}}}|\nu_{J_{v_{k}^{U}}}\cdot\xi|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+(M_{1}+|U|)\kappa\geq 2\int_{U\cap\Gamma}|\nu_{\Gamma}\cdot\xi|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\\
-2P(A,U)-2\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E])

Let
\phi^{o}
be the dual norm to
\phi,
i.e.,

\phi^{o}(\xi)=\sup\limits_{\phi(\nu)=1}|\xi\cdot\nu|.

Then

|\xi\cdot\nu|\leq\phi^{o}(\xi)\phi(\nu)

and hence, by (
4.23
) and the arbitrariness of
\kappa
we get

\displaystyle\phi^{o}(\xi)\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\int_{U\cap J_{v_{k}^{U}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{v_{k}^{U}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\geq 2\int_{U\cap\Gamma}|\nu_{\Gamma}\cdot\xi|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

(4.24)

\displaystyle-2P(A,U)-2\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E]).

Now using
\phi^{o}(\xi)\geq 1/b_{2}
from (
4
) we get
(
4.14
).

Step 3.
Now we prove (
(i)
).

Substep 3.1.
Let

\mathbb{S}_{\phi^{o}}^{n-1}:=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\,\,\phi^{o}(\xi)=1\}.

Since
\mathbb{S}_{\phi^{o}}^{n-1}
is compact,

\phi(\eta)=\max\limits_{i\geq 1}\eta\cdot\xi_{i}

for any countable set

\{\xi_{j}\}_{j}\subset\mathbb{S}_{\phi^{o}}^{n-1}
dense in
\mathbb{S}_{\phi^{o}}^{n-1}.

Fix any such dense set
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j}\subset\mathbb{S}_{\phi^{o}}^{n-1}
that if
\xi=\xi_{j}/|\xi_{j}|\in\Upsilon
, then (
4.15
) and (
4.16
) hold with
\xi.
By
[
23
, Lemma 6]
there exists a finite family
U_{1},\ldots,U_{m}
of disjoint open set compactly contained in
D
such that

(4.25)

2\int_{D\cap\Gamma}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}2\int_{U_{j}\cap\Gamma}|\nu_{\Gamma}\cdot\xi_{j}|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+\delta.

Recalling the definition of
(B_{k}^{U},u_{k}^{U})
from Step 2, let us define

B_{k}=\bigcap\limits_{j=1}^{m}B_{k}^{U_{j}}\quad\text{and}\quad v_{k}:=u_{k}\chi_{B_{k}\cup S}.

Then by (
\rm a_{2}
)
B_{k}\subset A_{k},

A_{k}\setminus B_{k}\subset\subset D
and

|A_{k}\setminus B_{k}|\leq\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}|U_{j}\cap(A_{k}\setminus B_{k}^{U_{j}})|\leq\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\frac{|U_{j}|}{k}\leq\frac{|D|}{k}.

Let
\Lambda_{k}(D)
be defined as in (
\rm a_{4}
) of Step 2 with
(B_{k},v_{k})
in place of
(B_{k}^{U},v_{k}^{U})
. Then by the definition of
(B_{k},v_{k}),

\alpha_{k}(D)
and (
a_{4}
)

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}(D)-\Lambda_{k}(D)=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\big(\alpha_{k}(U_{j})-\Lambda_{k}(U_{j})\big)\geq-\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\frac{|U_{j}|}{k}\geq-\frac{|D|}{k}.

Thus,

(4.26)

\alpha_{k}(D)\geq\Lambda_{k}(D)-\frac{|D|}{k}.

Substep 3.2.
Now we estimate
\Lambda_{k}(D)
from below.
Note that if
\xi_{j}/|\xi_{j}|\in\Upsilon,
then since
\phi^{o}(\xi_{j})=1,
by (
4.14
)

(4.27)

\displaystyle 2\int_{U_{j}\cap\Gamma}|\nu_{\Gamma}\cdot\xi_{j}|

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\Lambda_{k}(U_{j})+2b_{2}P(A,U_{j})+2b_{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U_{j}\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E]).

Now assume that
\xi:=\xi_{j}/|\xi_{j}|\notin\Upsilon.
Then by the definition of
\Upsilon
and (a3)

|\nu_{\Gamma}(x)\cdot\xi|\leq|(\nu_{\Gamma}(x)-{\bf e}_{n})\cdot\xi|+|{\bf e}_{n}\cdot\xi|<3\delta

for any
x\in U_{j}\cap\Gamma.
Thus,

(4.28)

2\int_{U_{j}\cap\Gamma}|\nu_{\Gamma}\cdot\xi_{j}|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq 6\delta\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U_{j}\cap\Gamma).

Now by (
4.25
), (
4.27
) and (
4.28
)

2\int_{D\cap\Gamma}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq\delta+\sum\limits_{j=1,\,j\in\Upsilon}^{m}\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\,\Lambda_{k}(U_{j})+6\delta\sum\limits_{j=1,\,j\notin\Upsilon}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U_{j}\cap\Gamma)\\
+2b_{2}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\Big[P(A,U_{j})+2\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U_{j}\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E])\Big].

Since set function
Q\mapsto\Lambda_{k}(Q)
is additive and non-increasing and the family
\{U_{j}\}
is pairwise disjoint,

\sum\limits_{j=1,\,j\in\Upsilon}^{m}\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\,\Lambda_{k}(U_{j})\leq\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\Lambda_{k}(\cup_{j}U_{j})\leq\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\Lambda_{k}(D).

Moreover, by (a2)

\sum\limits_{j=1,\,j\notin\Upsilon}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(U_{j}\cap\Gamma)\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap\Gamma)<1+\delta,

and by (a2), (a5), (a7.2) and (a7.3)

\sum\limits_{j=1}^{n}\Big[P(A,U_{j})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap[\Gamma\setminus\partial^{*}E])\Big]\\
\leq P(A,Q_{1})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap\Gamma)-\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap\Gamma\cap\partial^{*}E)\leq 6\delta.

Then

\displaystyle 2\int_{D\cap\Gamma}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq

\displaystyle\delta+\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\Lambda_{k}(D)+6\delta(1+\delta)+6b_{2}\delta,

and hence,

(4.29)

\displaystyle\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\Lambda_{k}(D)\geq 2\int_{D\cap\Gamma}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c_{0}\delta,

where

c_{0}:=13+6b_{2}

depends only on
b_{2}.

Substep 3.3.
From (
4.26
) and (
4.29
) there exist
k_{0}:=k_{0}(\delta,b_{2})>0
such that

(4.30)

\Lambda_{k}(D)\geq 2\int_{D\cap\Gamma}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-2c_{0}\delta

for all
k>k_{0}.
Since
|D|<|Q|=1,
one has
|D|/k<c_{0}\delta
provided
k>\frac{1}{c_{0}\delta}.
Let

k_{\delta}^{\prime}:=\max\Big\{k_{0},\frac{1}{c_{0}\delta}\Big\}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\int_{D\cap\Gamma}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\geq\int_{D\cap\Gamma\cap\partial^{*}E}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

Moreover, by (a7.3)

\int_{D\cap\partial^{*}E\setminus\Gamma}\phi(\nu_{E})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq b_{2}\delta

and by Lemma
3.1
(ii)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap\partial^{*}E\setminus D)<2\delta,

and therefore,

\int_{D\cap\Gamma}\phi(\nu_{\Gamma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\geq\int_{Q_{1}\cap\partial^{*}E}\phi(\nu_{E})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-3b_{2}\delta\qquad\text{for all $k>k_{\delta}^{\prime}$}.

Combining these estimates with (
4.26
) and (
4.30
) we deduce

\alpha_{k}(D)\geq 2\int_{K}\phi(\nu_{K})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-(2c_{0}+6b_{2})\delta.

Hence,

c^{\prime}:=c_{b_{2}}^{\prime}=(2c_{0}+6b_{2})

satisfies the assertion.
∎

4.1.
Lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{F}_{p}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\rm Dir}

We conclude this section by showing that the functionals
\mathcal{F}_{p}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\rm Dir}
in Theorems
2.8
and
2.9
, respectively, are lower semicontinuous with respect to the
\tau
-convergence defined in (
2.18
).
Indeed, the proof of the
\tau
-lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{S}
in
\mathcal{C}_{p}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\rm Dir}
is exactly the same as the
\tau_{\mathcal{C}}
-lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{S}
in
\mathcal{C}
(see the proof of Theorem
2.5
). To prove the
\tau
-lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{W}_{p}
and
\mathcal{W}_{\rm Dir}
we notice that according to the proof of the density estimate (
4.7f
), we only need the convexity of
W_{p}(x,\cdot)
and the weak convergence of
\mathcal{E}u_{k}
to
\mathcal{E}u
in
L^{p}({\rm Int}{(\Omega\cup S\cup\Sigma)});
the first condition is already stated in the assumption (a1) of
W_{p}
and the second condition follows from the lower bound in (a2) and the compactness result
[
14
, Theorem 1.1]
.

5.
Compactness in
\mathcal{C}

In this section we prove Theorem
2.6
. Note that if
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}
is an energy-equibounded sequence, then by a priori estimates (see Remark
2.3
) we can find a set of finite perimeter
A\subset\Omega
such that, up to a subsequence,
A_{k}\to A
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).
Moreover, since each connected component
S_{i}
of
S
is Lipschitz, the convergence of
u_{k}
in
S_{i}
can be obtained by adding rigid displacements in
S_{i}.
However, since the rigid displacements for
S_{i}
may differ from those for
S_{j},

j\neq i,
we need to create extra jumps for the resulting displacement field. Hence, as in
[
36
]
we need to partition
A_{k}
to compensate those jumps. The following proposition provides such a partition up to some error.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
(A_{k},u_{k}),(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}
be admissible configurations,
S^{i}
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
be a nonempty union of some connected components of
S
such that
S^{i}\cap S^{j}=\emptyset
and
S=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}S^{i},

\{a_{k}^{1}\},\ldots,\{a_{k}^{m}\}
be sequences of rigid displacements,
u^{1},\ldots,u^{m}\in GSBD^{2}({\rm Int}{(\Omega\cup S\cup\Sigma)})
and

F^{1},\ldots,F^{m}\subset A
be pairwise disjoint sets of finite perimeter. Assume that

•

\sup_{k}\mathcal{F}(A_{k},u_{k})<+\infty
and
A_{k}\to A
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n});

•

for any
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
one has
u_{k}-a_{k}^{i}\to u^{i}
a.e. in
S^{i}\cup F^{i}
and

|u_{k}-a_{k}^{i}|\to+\infty
a.e.
(S\setminus S^{i})\cup(A\setminus F^{i}).

Then for any
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{8}\,\min_{i\neq j}\{1,\mathrm{dist}(S^{i},S^{j})\})
there exist a (not relabelled) subsequence
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\},

k_{\delta}>0,

s_{\delta}\in(0,\delta)

and a sequence
\{G_{k}^{\delta}\}\subset BV(\Omega;\{0,1\})

such that

(5.1a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}([A_{k}\setminus A]^{(1)}\cap\{\mathrm{dist}(\cdot,S)=s_{\delta}\})<c^{*}\delta,

(5.1b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\{\mathrm{dist}(\cdot,S)<s_{\delta}\}\cap\partial^{*}A)<c^{*}\delta,

(5.1c)

\displaystyle|G_{k}^{\delta}|<c^{*}\sqrt{\delta}\sum_{0\leq i\leq m}P(F^{i}),

(5.1d)

\displaystyle P(G_{k}^{\delta})\leq c^{*}\sum_{0\leq i\leq m}P(F^{i}),

and the sequence
\{(B_{k}^{\delta},v_{k}^{\delta})\},
defined as

(5.2)

B_{k}^{\delta}:=A_{k}\setminus G_{k}^{\delta}

and

(5.3)

v_{k}^{\delta}:=\begin{cases}u_{k}-a_{k}^{i}&\text{in $S^{i}\cup[F^{i}\setminus G_{k}^{\delta}]\cup[R_{\delta}^{i}\cap(B_{k}^{\delta}\setminus A)]$ for $i=1,\ldots,m,$}\\
u_{0}&\text{in $B_{k}^{\delta}\cap F^{0},$}\\
\xi&\text{in $(\Omega\setminus B_{k}^{\delta})\cup(B_{k}^{\delta}\setminus[A\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}R_{\delta}^{i}]),$}\end{cases}

where
\xi\in(0,1)^{n},

R_{\delta}^{i}:=\{x\in\Omega:\,\,\mathrm{dist}(x,S^{i})<s_{\delta}\},\qquad F^{0}:=A\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}F^{i},

satisfies

(5.4)

\mathcal{S}(A_{k},u_{k})\geq\mathcal{S}(B_{k}^{\delta},v_{k}^{\delta})-c^{*}\sqrt{\delta}\Big[1+P(A_{k})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{k}})+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{i})\Big]

for all
k>k_{\delta}.
Here constant
c^{*}>0
depends only on
n,

b_{1}
and
b_{2}.

Figure 3.
The partition of
A=\bigcup_{i\geq 0}F^{i}
and the construction of
B_{k}^{\delta}:=A_{k}\setminus G_{k}^{\delta}
in Proposition
5.1
. The set
G_{\delta,k}
is a finite union of holes along the boundaries
F^{i}\cup\bigcup_{j\neq i}S^{j}
in which
u_{k}-a_{k}^{i}
converges. Note that the sets
\{F^{i}\setminus G_{k}^{\delta}\}_{i=0}^{m}
partition
B_{k}^{\delta}.
Since
F^{0}
is a “hanging” component of
A,
i.e., not linked to the substrate, and hence, it is reasonable to assume that the elastic energy in
F^{0}
is
0.
Then we define the displacement fields
v_{k}^{\delta}
as follows: in
S^{i}\cup(F^{i}\setminus G_{k}^{\delta})
for
i=1,\ldots,m
we set
v_{k}^{\delta}:=u_{k}-a_{k}^{i}
and in
F^{0}\setminus G_{k}^{\delta}
we write
v_{k}^{\delta}:=u_{0}.
Finally, since
A_{k}\setminus A
may present large trace portions along
\partial S
on which
v_{k}^{\delta}
forms a jump, we need to change the values of
v_{k}^{\delta}
in
R_{\delta}^{i}\setminus A
near
S^{i}.

We postpone the proof of Proposition
5.1
after the proof of Theorem
2.6
.

Proof of Theorem
2.6
.

Since
S
is Lipschitz open set with finitely many connected components, applying the Poincaré-Korn inequality and the Rellich-Kondrachov compactness theorem we find a not relabelled subsequence
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\},
a partition
\{S^{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
of
S
and
m
sequences
\{a_{k}^{1}\},\ldots,\{a_{k}^{m}\}
of rigid displacements such that

(
\rm a_{1}
)

each
S^{i}
is the union of some connected components of
S
and
S=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}S^{i};

(
\rm a_{2}
)

for each
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
there exists
w^{i}\in H^{1}(S^{i})
such that
u_{k}-a_{k}^{i}
converges to
w^{i}
weakly in
H^{1}(S^{i})
and a.e. in
S^{i};

(
\rm a_{3}
)

if
i\neq j,
then
|a_{k}^{i}-a_{k}^{j}|\to+\infty
a.e. in
\mathbb{R}^{n}.

We may also assume
A_{k}\to A
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for some
A\in BV(\Omega;\{0,1\}).

Since
\mathcal{E}v=\mathcal{E}(v+a)
for any rigid displacement
a
, by Remark
2.3
we have

\sup_{k\geq 1}\Big(P(A_{k})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{(u_{k}-a_{k}^{i})\chi_{A_{k}\cup S}})+\int_{A_{k}\cup S}|\mathcal{E}(u_{k}-a_{k}^{i})|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\Big)<+\infty

for any
i.
Hence, by
[
14
, Theorem 1.1]
there exist a not relabelled subsequence
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}
such that for each
i
the set

F_{i}:=\{x\in\Omega:\,\,\limsup\limits_{k\to+\infty}|(u_{k}(x)-a_{k}^{i}(x))\chi_{A_{k}}(x)|=+\infty\}

has finite perimeter and there exists a function
u^{i}\in GSBD^{2}({\rm Int}{(\Omega\cup S\cup\Sigma)})
such that

\displaystyle u_{k}-a_{k}^{i}\to u^{i}\quad\text{a.e.\ in $S^{i}\cup F^{i},$}

where

F^{i}:=A\setminus F_{i}.

By assumption (
\rm a_{3}
) the sets
F^{1},\ldots,F^{m}
are pairwise disjoint
(see Figure
3
).

Let
\delta_{0}:=2^{-10}\min_{i\neq j}\{1,\mathrm{dist}(S^{i},S^{j})\}
and consider any sequence
\delta_{l}\searrow 0
with
\delta_{1}<\delta_{0}.
By Proposition
5.1
for any
l\geq 1
there exists a subsequence
\{(A_{k,l},u_{k,l})\}_{k}\subset\{(A_{k,l-1},u_{k,l-1})\}_{k},

k_{\delta_{l}}>0,

s_{\delta_{l}}\in(0,\delta_{l})
and a sequence
\{G_{k}^{\delta_{l}}\}_{k}
of sets of finite perimeter satisfying (
5.1a
)-(
5.1d
) with
\delta=\delta_{l}
such that the sequence
\{(B_{k}^{\delta_{l}},v_{k}^{\delta_{l}})\}_{k},
defined as (
5.2
)-(
5.3
), satisfies

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(A_{k,l},u_{k,l})\geq\mathcal{S}(B_{k}^{\delta_{l}},v_{k}^{\delta_{l}})-c^{*}\sqrt{\delta_{l}}\Big[1+P(A_{k,l})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{k,l}})+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{i})\Big]

for all
k>k_{\delta_{l}}.
Here we set
(A_{k,0},u_{k,0})=(A_{k},u_{k}).
By (
5.1d
) we may also assume that
G_{k}^{\delta_{l}}\to G^{\delta_{l}}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
as
k\to+\infty,
and therefore,
B_{k}^{\delta_{l}}\to A\setminus G^{\delta_{l}}.

Moreover, setting
v_{k}^{\delta_{l}}=\xi
in
\Omega\setminus B_{k}^{\delta_{l}}
and
B_{k}^{\delta_{l}}\setminus[\cup_{i}R_{\delta_{l}}^{i}\cup A]
for some
\xi\in(0,1)^{n}\setminus\Xi_{\{B_{k}^{\delta_{l}},u_{k}^{\delta_{l}}\}_{k,l}}
(see Remark
2.1
), by the choice of
a_{k}^{i}
we get

v_{k}^{\delta_{l}}\to v^{\delta_{l}}

a.e. in
\Omega\cup S,
where

v^{\delta_{l}}:=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}u^{i}\chi_{S^{i}\cup(F^{i}\setminus G^{\delta_{l}})}+u_{0}\chi_{F^{0}\setminus G^{1/l}}+\xi\chi_{(\Omega\setminus A)\cup G^{1/l}}.

By (
5.1c
)-(
5.1d
)

|G^{\delta_{l}}|\leq c^{*}\sqrt{\delta_{l}}\sum_{i=0}^{m}P(F^{i}),\qquad P(G^{\delta_{l}})\leq c^{*}\sum_{i=0}^{m}P(F^{i}),

and hence,
G^{\delta_{l}}\to\emptyset
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
as
l\to+\infty.
Therefore,
v^{\delta_{l}}\to u
a.e. in
\Omega\cup S
as
l\to+\infty,
where

u:=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}u^{i}\chi_{S^{i}\cup F^{i}}+u_{0}\chi_{F^{0}}+\xi\chi_{\Omega\setminus A}.

By the nonnegativity and invariance w.r.t. rigid displacements of the elastic energy we have also

(5.6)

\mathcal{W}(A_{k,l},u_{k,l})\geq\mathcal{W}(B_{k}^{\delta_{l}},v_{k}^{\delta_{l}}).

For each
l\geq 1
let us choose
k_{l}>k_{\delta_{l}}
and consider the sequences
\{(A_{k_{l},l},u_{k_{l},l})\}_{l}
and let
(B_{l},v_{l}):=(B_{k_{l}}^{l},u_{k_{l}}^{l}).
We may also assume that
l\mapsto k_{l}
is strictly increasing. By construction and the definition of
u,
one readily check that

(B_{l},v_{l})\overset{\tau_{\mathcal{C}}}{\longrightarrow}(A,u).
Moreover, by construction and (
5.1c
)
|A_{k_{l},l}\Delta B_{l}|=|G_{k_{l}}^{\delta_{l}}|\to 0.

Finally, from (
5.5
) and (
5.6
) we immediately get

\liminf\limits_{l\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}(A_{k_{l},l},u_{k_{l},l})\geq\liminf\limits_{l\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}(B_{l},u_{l}).

Thus, the subsequence
\{(A_{k_{l},l},u_{k_{l},l})\}_{l},
the sequence
\{(B_{l},u_{l})\}
and the configuration
(A,u)
satisfy the assertions of Theorem
2.6
.
∎

Note that by construction
|B_{l}|\leq|A_{k_{l}}|
and hence, in general our technique does not imply the compactness of energy-equibounded sequences
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}
satisfying a volume constraint.

5.1.
Proof of Proposition
5.1

We start with the following estimates near the points of reduced boundary of
A
(in Proposition
5.1
).

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1/8),

U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be an open set,
E_{k},E\in BV(U;\{0,1\}),
and
Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\subset\subset U,

r>0,

\nu\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1},
be a cube such that

(a1)

x_{0}\in\partial^{*}E,

\nu_{E}(x_{0})=\nu
and

1-\delta<\frac{1}{\phi(\nu)r^{n-1}}\int_{Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\partial^{*}E}\phi(\nu_{E})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}<1+\delta;

(a2)

\Big(\frac{1}{2}-\delta\Big)r^{n}<|E\cap Q_{r,\nu}^{-}(x_{0})|,|E\cap Q_{r,\nu}^{+}(x_{0})|<\Big(\frac{1}{2}+\delta\Big)r^{n},

where

Q_{r,\nu}^{\pm}(x_{0})=\{x\in Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0}):\,\,(x-x_{0})\cdot\nu\gtrless 0\};

(a3)

E_{k}\to E
in
L^{1}(U).

We also denote by
\phi
a norm in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying (
4.1
).
Then there exists
k_{\delta}>0
such that for any
k>k_{\delta}
there is
t_{k}^{\delta}\in(\sqrt{\delta},2\sqrt{\delta})
such that
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{t_{k}^{\delta}r}\cap\partial^{*}E_{k})=0
and

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{t_{k}^{\delta}r}\cap E_{k}^{(1)})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}r}\cap(Q_{1}^{-}\setminus E^{(1)}))+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}r}\cap(E_{k}^{(1)}\Delta E^{(1)}))<4\sqrt{\delta}r^{n-1},

where

T_{t}:=\{x\in Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0}):\,\,(x-x_{0})\cdot\nu=t\},\quad t\in(-r,r),

and the set

D_{k}^{\delta}:=Q_{r,\nu}(x_{0})\cap\{|(x-x_{0})\cdot\nu|<t_{k}^{\delta}r\}

satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{D_{k}^{\delta}\cap\partial^{*}E_{k}}\phi(\nu_{E_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\geq

\displaystyle\phi(\nu)\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}r})-(4n+12)b_{2}\sqrt{\delta}r^{n-1}.

(see Figure
4
).

In the proof of Proposition
5.1
we apply this proposition with
U=\Omega,

E_{k}:=A_{k}
and
E=A.

Figure 4.
The sets
E_{k}
and
E
in Proposition
5.2
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we assume that
x_{0}=0,

\nu={\bf e}_{n}
and
r=1.

By (a2)

|Q_{1}^{+}\cap E|\leq|E|-|E\cap Q_{1}^{-}|<2\delta,

and hence, by (a3) there exists
k_{\delta}>0
such that

(5.7)

|Q_{1}^{+}\cap E_{k}|<2\delta\quad\text{and}\quad|E_{k}\Delta E|<\delta\quad\text{for all $k>k_{\delta}.$ }

Also by (a2)

|Q_{1}^{-}\setminus E|\leq|Q_{1}^{-}|-|Q_{1}^{-}\cap E|<\delta,

thus, by (
5.7
) and the coarea formula

4\delta>|Q_{1}^{+}\cap E_{k}|+|Q_{1}^{-}\setminus E|+|E_{k}\Delta E|=\int_{0}^{1/2}\Big[\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{t}\cap E_{k}^{(1)})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t}\cap[Q_{1}^{-}\setminus E^{(1)}])\\
+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{t}\cap[E_{k}^{(1)}\Delta E^{(1)}])+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t}\cap[E_{k}^{(1)}\Delta E^{(1)}])\Big]\mathrm{d}t.

In particular there exists
t_{k}^{\delta}\in(\sqrt{\delta},2\sqrt{\delta})
such that

(5.8)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{t_{k}^{\delta}}\cap E_{k}^{(1)})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}}\cap(Q_{1}^{-}\setminus E^{(1)}))+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}}\cap(E_{k}^{(1)}\Delta E^{(1)}))<4\sqrt{\delta}.

Define

D_{k}^{\delta}:=\big(-1/2,1/2\big)^{n-1}\times(-t_{k}^{\delta},t_{k}^{\delta})

(see Figure
4
).
Note that

\int_{D_{k}^{\delta}\cap\partial^{*}E_{k}}\phi(\nu_{E_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=\int_{\{x\cdot{\bf e}_{n}>-t_{k}^{\delta}\}\cap\partial^{*}(D_{k}^{\delta}\cap E_{k})}\phi(\nu_{D_{k}^{\delta}\cap E_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\\
-\int_{\partial^{*}E_{k}\cap\overline{D_{k}^{\delta}}\cap\partial Q_{1}}\phi(\nu_{Q_{1}})\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-\int_{E_{k}^{(1)}\cap T_{t_{k}^{\delta}}}\phi({\bf e}_{n})\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

By the choice (
5.8
) of
t_{k}^{\delta}

\int_{E_{k}^{(1)}\cap T_{t_{k}^{\delta}}}\phi({\bf e}_{n})\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq b_{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(E_{k}^{(1)}\cap T_{t_{k}^{\delta}})<4b_{2}\sqrt{\delta}

and

\int_{\partial^{*}E_{k}\cap\overline{D_{k}^{\delta}}\cap\partial Q_{1}}\phi(\nu_{Q_{1}})\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq b_{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial D_{k}^{\delta}\cap\partial Q_{1})<4(n-1)b_{2}\sqrt{\delta},

where
2(n-1)
is the perimeter of
(-1/2,1/2)^{n-1}.

Moreover, by the anisotropic (local) minimality of half-spaces (see e.g.
[
7
, Example 2.4]
)

\int_{\{x\cdot{\bf e}_{n}>-t_{k}^{\delta}\}\cap\partial^{*}(D_{k}\cap E_{k})}\phi(\nu_{D_{k}\cap E_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\geq\phi({\bf e}_{n})\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(E_{k}^{(1)}\cap T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}}),

and hence, by (
5.8
) (we can replace
E_{k}
with

E
)

\int_{\{x\cdot{\bf e}_{n}>-t_{k}^{\delta}\}\cap\partial^{*}(D_{k}\cap E_{k})}\phi(\nu_{D_{k}\cap E_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\geq\phi({\bf e}_{n})\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(E^{(1)}\cap T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}})-4b_{2}\sqrt{\delta}.

Again by (
5.8
)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(E^{(1)}\cap T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}})=\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}})-\mathcal{H}^{n-1}((Q_{1}^{-}\setminus E^{(1)})\cap T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}})>\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}})-4\sqrt{\delta}

and therefore,

\int_{D_{k}^{\delta}\cap\partial^{*}E_{k}}\phi(\nu_{E_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\geq\phi({\bf e}_{n})\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k}^{\delta}})-4(n+3)b_{2}\sqrt{\delta}.

∎

Now applying Proposition
4.1
and
5.2
we construct the set
G_{k}^{\delta}
in Proposition
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Without loss of generality we assume
u_{k}=\xi
in
\Omega\setminus A_{k}
for some
\xi\in(0,1)^{n}\setminus\Xi_{\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}\}}
(see Remark
2.1
).

By the uniform continuity of
\varphi,
there exists
r_{\delta}\in(0,1)
such that

(5.9)

|\varphi(x,\nu)-\varphi(y,\nu)|<\delta\quad\text{for all $x,y\in\overline{\Omega}$ with $|x-y|<r_{\delta}.$}

Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{1}:=

\displaystyle\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A\cap\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\Big(\partial S^{i}\cap\bigcup_{j\neq i}\partial^{*}F^{j}\Big),

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{2}:=

\displaystyle\Omega\cap A^{(1)}\cap\bigcup_{i=0}^{m}\partial^{*}F^{i},

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{3}:=

\displaystyle\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A\cap\bigcup_{i=0}^{m}\partial^{*}F^{i}.

Since these sets are
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-rectifiable and pairwise disjoint, (by a simple covering argument) we can find open sets
U_{1}\subset\subset{\rm Int}{(\Omega\cup S\cup\Sigma)}
and
U_{2},U_{3}\subset\subset\Omega
with disjoint closures such that

(5.10)

\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\big(\widetilde{K}_{i}\setminus U_{i}\big)+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(\widetilde{K}_{i}\cap\bigcup_{j\neq i}U_{j}\Big)<\delta.

Set

\displaystyle K_{i}:=U_{i}\cap\widetilde{K}_{i},\quad i=1,2,3.

Note that around
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e. point of
\cup_{i}K_{i}
there exist
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and a cube
Q
such that
\cup_{i}K_{i}
“roughly divides”
Q
into two parts in one
u_{k}-a_{k}^{j}
converges and in the other either
u_{k}
is constant or
|u_{k}-a_{k}^{j}|\to+\infty.

For convenience of the reader we divide the construction of
G_{k}^{\delta}
into smaller steps.

Step 1.
Using the
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-rectifiability of
K_{i},

\partial^{*}A,

\partial^{*}F^{i},
the lipschitzianity of
\Sigma
and the Borel regularity of corresponding unit normals we construct a fine cover of
\cup_{i}K_{i}
as follows.

Substep 1.1: fine cover for
K_{1}.
For
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-a.e.

);

(

)

\theta(\Sigma,x)=\theta(K_{1},x)=\theta(\partial^{*}F^{j_{x}},x)=\theta(\partial^{*}A,x)=1

\nu_{F^{j_{x}}}(x)
and
\nu_{A}(x)

(

separates

into two connected components;

(

)

for any

(5.11a)

\displaystyle|\nu_{\Gamma_{x}}(y)-\nu_{x}|<\delta\quad\text{and}\quad|(y-x)\cdot\nu_{x}|<\tfrac{\delta r}{2}\quad\text{for all $y\in\Gamma_{x},$}

(5.11b)

\displaystyle(1-\delta)r^{n-1}<\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}\cap\Gamma_{x}\cap\partial^{*}F^{j_{x}})\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}\cap\Gamma_{x})<(1+\delta)r^{n-1},

(5.11c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(\Big[Q_{r}\cap\bigcup_{j=0}^{m}\partial^{*}F^{j}\Big]\setminus\Gamma_{x}\Big)+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}\cap[\partial^{*}F^{j_{x}}\Delta\Gamma_{x}])<\delta r^{n-1},

(5.11d)

\displaystyle|(F^{j_{x}}\cup S)\cap Q_{r}|\geq(1-\delta)r^{n},

where
Q_{r}:=Q_{r,\nu_{x}}(x).

Removing an
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-negligible set from
K_{1}
if necessary we assume that for all points
x\in K_{1}
there exist
r_{x}
and
i_{x},j_{x}
satisfying (
a_{1.1}
)-(
a_{1.4}
).

Let us show that for any

and

, the cube

, the sequence

and

Substep 1.2: fine cover for

\displaystyle(1-\delta)r^{n-1}<\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}\cap\Gamma_{x}\cap K_{2}\cap\partial^{*}F^{i_{x}}\cap\partial^{*}F^{j_{x}})

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}\cap\Gamma_{x})<(1+\delta)r^{n-1},

(5.12d)

\displaystyle\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}-\delta\Big)r^{n}\leq|F^{i_{x}}\cap Q_{r}^{-}|,|F^{j_{x}}\cap Q_{r}^{+}|\leq\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}+\delta\Big)r^{n},

Here the volume density estimates follows from the definition of reduced boundary.

\displaystyle(1-\delta)r^{n-1}<\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}\cap\Gamma_{x}\cap K_{3}\cap\partial^{*}F^{i_{x}}\cap\partial^{*}A)

(5.13b)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}\cap\Gamma_{x})<(1+\delta)r^{n-1},

(5.13c)

(5.13d)

(5.13e)

(5.13f)

\displaystyle\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}-\delta\Big)r^{n}<|Q_{r}^{-}\cap F^{i_{x}}|\leq|Q_{r}^{-}\cap A|<\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}+\delta\Big)r^{n},

(5.13g)

\displaystyle|Q_{r}^{+}\cap A|<\delta r^{n},

where
Q_{r}:=Q_{r,\nu_{x}}(x).

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-negligible set from

)-(

. Indeed, conditions (a1)-(a2) are given in (

in

as

Figure 5.
Construction of holes
C_{1}^{j},

C_{2}^{j}
and
D_{k}^{j}.

Step 2.
Now we extract finitely many covering cubes still covering
\cup_{i}K_{i}
up to some error of order
O(\sqrt{\delta}),
and create “holes” inside those cubes (i.e., the sets
C_{1}^{j},

C_{2}^{j}
and
D_{k}^{j}
in Figure
5
). By Step 1, for each
i\in\{1,2,3\}
the collection
\{\overline{Q_{r,\nu_{x}}(x)}:\,\,x\in K_{i},\,\,r\in(0,r_{x})\}
of cubes provides a fine cover for
K_{i}
and hence, by the Vitali covering lemma we can extract an at most countable pairwise disjoint family
\{Q_{r_{j}^{i},\nu_{x_{j}^{i}}}(x_{j}^{i}),\,x_{j}^{i}\in K_{i}\}
such that

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(K_{i}\setminus\bigcup_{j}Q_{r_{j}^{i},\nu_{x_{j}^{i}}}(x_{j}^{i})\Big)=0.

Since
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(K_{i})<+\infty
, there exists
N_{i}\geq 1
such that

(5.14)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(K_{i}\setminus\bigcup_{j>N_{i}}Q_{r_{j}^{i},\nu_{x_{j}^{i}}}(x_{j}^{i})\Big)<\delta.

Moreover, decreasing
r_{j}
a bit necessary, we assume that
\overline{Q_{r_{j}^{i},\nu_{x_{j}^{i}}}(x_{j}^{i})}\cap\overline{Q_{r_{j^{\prime}}^{i},\nu_{x_{j^{\prime}}^{i}}}(x_{j^{\prime}}^{i})}=\emptyset
for all
1\leq j<j^{\prime}\leq N_{i}.

Since
\overline{U_{i}}\cap\overline{U_{j}}=\emptyset
for
i\neq j,
cubes belonging to the union of
\mathcal{G}_{i}:=\{Q_{r_{j}^{i},\nu_{x_{j}^{i}}}(x_{j}^{i})\}_{j=1}^{N_{i}},

i=1,2,3,
have disjoint closures. When no confusion arises, we drop the dependence of
x_{j}^{i}
and
r_{j}^{i}
on
i.

Substep 2.1: definition of
C_{1}^{j}.
Let
Q_{r_{j},x_{j}}(x_{j})\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
for some
j\in\{1,\ldots,N_{1}\}.
By Substep 1.1
x_{j}\in K_{1}\cap\partial S^{l_{j}}\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}
for some
l_{j},h_{j}\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
with
l_{j}\neq h_{j}.
Applying
Proposition
4.1
(ii) with
Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\subset\subset{\rm Int}{(\Omega\cup S\cup\Sigma)},

\Gamma_{x_{j}}:=Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap\Sigma,

\{(A_{k},u_{k}-a_{k}^{h_{j}})\},

(A,u^{h_{j}}),

E:=Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\setminus F^{h_{j}},

K:=Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}
and
\phi(\cdot)=\varphi(x_{j},\cdot)
we find an open set
C_{1}^{j}\subset\Omega\cap Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})
of finite perimeter (given by Lemma
3.1
) and
k_{\delta}^{1,j}>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{C_{1}^{j}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{C_{1}^{j}\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\int_{Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}}\phi(\nu_{F^{h_{j}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime}\delta r_{j}^{n-1}

(5.15)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j}}\phi(\nu_{C_{1}^{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-(c^{\prime}+5b_{2})\delta r_{j}^{n-1}

for all
k>k_{\delta}^{1,j}
and for some
c^{\prime}>0
(depending only on
b_{2}
).

Let us estimate the perimeter and the volume of
\cup_{j}C_{1}^{j}.
By (
5.11b
)

(5.16)

r_{j}^{n-1}\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})

and hence, by (
2.8
) and (
3.2
)

\displaystyle b_{1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j})\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j}}\phi(\nu_{C_{1}^{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq 2\int_{Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}}\phi(\nu_{F^{h_{j}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+5b_{2}\delta r_{j}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2b_{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})+5b_{2}\delta r_{j}^{n-1}

so that

(5.17)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j})\leq\frac{3b_{2}}{b_{1}}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}).

Moreover,

|C_{1}^{j}|\leq\delta r_{j}^{n}<\delta r_{j}^{n-1}\leq 2\delta\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})

and therefore,

(5.18)

\Big|\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{i}}C_{1}^{j}\Big|\leq 2\delta\sum\limits_{h=1}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{h}).

Let us estimate the error in covering
K_{1}
by
\{C_{1}^{j}\}.
Fix some
j\in\{1,\ldots,N_{1}\}.
Then by the definition of
K_{1},
the error estimate (
5.11c
) and Lemma
3.1
(ii)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}((Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap K_{1})\setminus\overline{C_{1}^{j}})\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(\Big[Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap\bigcup_{j=0}^{N_{1}}\partial^{*}F^{j}\Big]\setminus\Gamma_{x_{j}}\Big)+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\Gamma_{x_{j}}\setminus\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})\\
+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}([Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}]\setminus\overline{C_{1}^{j}})<3\delta r_{j}^{n-1}

and thus, by (
5.16
) and the choice
\delta<1/8

(5.19)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}((Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap K_{1})\setminus\overline{C_{1}^{j}})<4\delta\,\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}).

From (
5.14
) and (
5.19
) it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(K_{1}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\overline{C_{1}^{j}}\Big)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(K_{1}\setminus\bigcup_{j>N_{1}}Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\Big)+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}([Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap K_{1}]\setminus\overline{C_{1}^{j}})

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\delta+4\delta\sum_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})

so that by the disjointness of
\{F^{h}\}

(5.20)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(K_{1}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\overline{C_{1}^{j}}\Big)<\delta+4\delta\sum\limits_{h=1}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{h}).

Substep 2.2: construction of
C_{2}^{j}
.
Let
Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\in\mathcal{G}_{2}
for some
j\in\{1,\ldots,N_{2}\}
so that there exist
l_{j},h_{j}\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
with
l_{j}\neq h_{j}\neq 0
such that
x_{j}\in\partial^{*}F^{l_{j}}\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}.
As in Substep 2.1 applying Proposition
4.1
with
Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\subset\subset\Omega,

\Gamma_{x_{j}},

\{(A_{k},u_{k}-a_{k}^{h_{j}})\},

(A,u^{h_{j}}),

E:=Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\setminus F^{h_{j}},

K:=Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}
and
\phi(\cdot)=\varphi(x_{j},\cdot)
we find an open set
C_{2}^{j}\subset\subset Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})
of finite perimeter (given by Lemma
3.1
) and
k_{\delta}^{2,j}>0
such that

(5.21)

\displaystyle\int_{C_{2}^{j}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{C_{2}^{j}\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\phi(\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\geq\int_{\partial C_{2}^{j}}\phi(\nu_{C_{2}^{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-c^{\prime}\delta r_{j}^{n-1}

for all
k>k_{\delta}^{2,j},
where
c^{\prime}
depends only on
b_{2}.
As in Substep 2.1,
by (
5.12b
)

(5.22)

r_{j}^{n-1}\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{l_{j}}\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})

by (
2.8
) and (
3.1
)

(5.23)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}C_{2}^{j})\leq\frac{2b_{2}}{b_{1}}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})+\frac{5b_{2}}{b_{1}}\,\delta r_{j}^{n-1}\leq\frac{3b_{2}}{b_{1}}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})

and

(5.24)

Moreover,

(5.25)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\Big(K_{2}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{2}}\overline{C_{2}^{j}}\Big)<\delta+4\delta\sum_{h=0}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{h})

Substep 2.3: construction of

.

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}}^{j}\cap A_{k}^{(1)})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}}^{j}\cap

\displaystyle[Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}^{-}(x_{j})\setminus A^{(1)}])

(5.26)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}}^{j}\cap[A_{k}^{(1)}\Delta A^{(1)}])<4\sqrt{\delta}r_{j}^{n-1},

where

T_{t}^{j}:=\{x\in Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j}):\,\,(x-x_{j})\cdot\nu_{j}=t\},\quad t\in(-r_{j},r_{j}),

and

Note that by (

)

r_{j}^{n-1}\leq\frac{1}{1-\delta}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}\cap\partial^{*}A)

\Big|\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{3}}D_{k}^{j}\Big|\leq 5\sqrt{\delta}\sum\limits_{h=1}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{h}).

Let us estimate the error in covering

Step 3: Definition of

.

for a.e.

In particular, there exists

such that

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}([A_{k}\Delta A]\cap\{\mathrm{dist}(\cdot,S)=s_{\delta}\}\})<\delta\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\{0<\mathrm{dist}(\cdot,S)<s_{\delta}\}\}\cap\partial^{*}A)<\delta,

Step 5: Proof of (
5.4
).
Let
B_{k}^{\delta}
and
v_{k}^{\delta}
be given by (
5.2
) and (
5.3
).
As in the proof of lower semicontinuity, given
(B,v)\in\mathcal{C}
and a Borel set
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{n},
let us introduce

\displaystyle\mu_{B,v}(D):=

\displaystyle\int_{D\cap\partial^{*}B}\varphi(x,\nu_{B})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{D\cap B^{(1)}\cap J_{v}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{v}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+2\int_{D\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}B\cap J_{v}}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+\int_{D\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}B\setminus J_{v}}[\beta+\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})]\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+\int_{D\cap\Sigma\setminus\partial^{*}B}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

Since

\mu_{B,v}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=\mathcal{S}(B,v)+\int_{\Sigma}\varphi(x,\nu_{\Sigma})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1},

we have

\mathcal{S}(A_{k},u_{k})-\mathcal{S}(B_{k}^{\delta},v_{k}^{\delta})=\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})-\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

By construction

\displaystyle[\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}]\setminus\overline{G_{k}^{\delta}}=[\Omega\cap(\partial^{*}B_{k}^{\delta}]\setminus\overline{G_{k}^{\delta}},\quad[\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}]\setminus\overline{G_{k}^{\delta}}=\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}B_{k}^{\delta},

\displaystyle[\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap J_{u_{k}}]\setminus\overline{G_{k}^{\delta}}=\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}B_{k}^{\delta}\cap J_{v_{k}^{\delta}},\quad\Sigma\setminus\Big(\partial^{*}A_{k}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j})=\Sigma\setminus\partial^{*}B_{k}^{\delta},

\displaystyle[A^{(1)}\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}]\setminus\overline{G_{k}^{\delta}}=A^{(1)}\cap B_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{v_{k}^{\delta}},

\displaystyle[A^{(1)}\cap J_{v_{k}^{\delta}}]\setminus J_{u_{k}}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\partial^{*}F^{j}\setminus G_{k}^{\delta},\quad J_{v_{k}^{\delta}}\cap\partial^{*}A\subset\bigcup_{j=0}^{m}\partial^{*}F^{j}\setminus G_{k}^{\delta},

\displaystyle[A_{k}^{(1)}\setminus A^{(1)}]\cap J_{v_{k}^{\delta}}\subseteq([R_{\delta}\setminus A]^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}})\cup([A_{k}\setminus A]^{(1)}\cap\partial R_{\delta})\cup(R_{\delta}\cap\partial^{*}A),

and hence,

(5.32)

\mathcal{S}(A_{k},u_{k})-\mathcal{S}(B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta})\geq\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\overline{G_{k}^{\delta}})-\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta}}(\overline{G_{k}^{\delta}})-2\int_{R_{\delta}\cap\partial^{*}A}\varphi(x,\nu_{A})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\\
-2\int_{J_{v_{k}^{\delta}}\cap[B_{k}^{\delta}]^{(1)}\cap\bigcup_{i=0}^{m}\partial^{*}F^{j}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{v_{k}^{\delta}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-2\int_{[A_{k}\setminus A]^{(1)}\cap\partial R_{\delta}}\varphi(x,\nu_{R_{\delta}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

By (
2.8
), the definition of
\widetilde{K}_{j}
and
U_{j},
the construction of
C_{1}^{j},C_{2}^{j},D_{k}^{j}
, the choice of
s_{\delta}
and the error estimates (
5.20
), (
5.25
), (
5.31
) and (
5.10
) we have

(5.33)

\int_{R_{\delta}\cap\partial^{*}A}\varphi(x,\nu_{A})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+\int_{J_{v_{k}^{\delta}}\cap[B_{k}^{\delta}]^{(1)}\cap\bigcup_{i=0}^{m}\partial^{*}F^{j}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{v_{k}^{\delta}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\\
+\int_{[A_{k}\setminus A]^{(1)}\cap\partial R_{\delta}}\varphi(x,\nu_{R_{\delta}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq c_{4}^{*}\delta\Big(1+\sum\limits_{h=0}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{h})\Big).

Furthermore, from the additivity of the set-function
\alpha_{B,v}
and disjointness of the closures of

and

we obtain

\displaystyle\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\overline{G_{k}^{\delta}})-\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta}}(\overline{G_{k}^{\delta}})=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{j=1}^{N_{1}}\Big[\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\overline{C_{1}^{j}})-\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta}}(\overline{C_{j}^{1}})\Big]+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{N_{2}}\Big[\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\overline{C_{2}^{j}})-\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta}}(\overline{C_{2}^{1}})\Big]

(5.34)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{N_{3}}\Big[\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\overline{D_{k}^{j}})-\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta}}(\overline{D_{k}^{1}})\Big]:=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

Substep 5.1: A lower estimate for
I_{1}.

Let

+2\int_{\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

By (
5.9
) and (

) we have

(5.35)

\alpha_{k}^{1,j}\geq\int_{\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j}}\varphi(x,\nu_{C_{1}^{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-\delta\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\overline{C_{1}^{j}}\cap[J_{u_{k}}\cup\partial^{*}A_{k}])-\delta\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j})-c^{\prime}\delta r_{j}^{n-1}.

Since

(see (

)), by the definition of

and

we have

Therefore, from (

) and (

) we get

-\delta\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}C_{1}^{j})-\frac{c^{\prime}\delta}{1-\delta}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}\cap\Sigma\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}}).

Summing these estimates in
j
and using the disjointness of
\{Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\}
and the perimeter estimate (
5.17
) of
C_{1}^{j}
we deduce

(5.36)

\displaystyle I_{1}\geq-c_{1}^{*}\delta\Big(\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{k}})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}A_{k})+\sum\limits_{h=0}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{h})\Big)

for all
k>k_{\delta}^{1}=\max_{j}k_{\delta}^{1,j}
and for some
c_{1}^{*}
depending only on
b_{1}
and
b_{2}.

Substep 5.2: A lower estimate for
I_{2}.
Let

\alpha_{k}^{2,j}:=\int_{C_{2}^{j}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\varphi(x,\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{C_{2}^{j}\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{k}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{u_{k}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

By (
5.9
) and (
5.21
)

(5.37)

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{2,j}\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial^{*}C_{2}^{j}}\varphi(x,\nu_{C_{2}^{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-\delta\,\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap[\partial^{*}A_{k}\cup J_{u_{k}}]-\delta\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}C_{2}^{j})-c^{\prime}\delta r_{j}^{n-1}

for all
k>k_{\delta}^{2,j}

Since
\overline{C_{2}^{j}}\cap\Sigma=\emptyset,
from the definition of
\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}},

(B_{k}^{\delta},v_{k}^{\delta})
and
\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},v_{k}^{\delta}}
we have

\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\overline{C_{2}^{j}})=\alpha_{k}^{2,j}\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\partial^{*}C_{2}^{j}}\varphi(x,\nu_{C_{2}^{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},v_{k}^{\delta}}(\overline{C_{2}^{j}})

and thus, using (

) and (

) in (

) we obtain

\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\overline{C_{2}^{j}})-\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},v_{k}^{\delta}}(\overline{C_{2}^{j}})\\
\geq-c_{2}^{*}\delta\Big(\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap[\partial^{*}A_{k}\cup J_{u_{k}}]+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{l_{j}}\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})\Big)

for some constant
c_{2}^{*}>0
depending only on

Summing these estimates we get

(5.38)

\displaystyle I_{2}\geq-c_{2}^{*}\delta\Big(\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{k}})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}A_{k})+\sum_{h=0}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{h})\Big)

for all

Substep 5.3: A lower estimate for
I_{3}.
Let

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{3,j}:=\int_{D_{k}^{j}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}\varphi(x,\nu_{A_{k}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}.

Since

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(T_{-t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}})r_{j}^{n-1},
using (
5.9
) and (
5.27
) we get

(5.39)

\alpha_{k}^{3,j}\geq\int_{Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap T_{-t_{k,j}^{\delta}r}}\varphi(x,\nu_{x_{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-(\delta+c^{\prime}\sqrt{\delta})r_{j}^{n-1}

for all
k>k_{\delta}^{3,j}.

Moreover, by the choice of
t_{k,j}^{\delta},
(
5.1
) and (
2.8
)

\displaystyle\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta}}(\overline{D_{k}^{j}})\leq

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap T_{-t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{x_{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+\int_{Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap A_{k}^{(1)}\cap T_{t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{x_{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+\int_{\partial^{*}D_{k}^{j}\setminus[T_{-t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}}\cup T_{t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}}]}\varphi(x,\nu_{D_{k}^{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r_{j},\nu_{x_{j}}}(x_{j})\cap T_{-t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{x_{j}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+4b_{2}\sqrt{\delta}r_{j}^{n-1}+2b_{2}t_{k,j}^{\delta}r_{j}^{n-1}..

Now using
t_{k,j}^{\delta}\leq 2\sqrt{\delta}
and (

) in this estimate and combining with (

) and obvious inequality
\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\overline{D_{k}^{j}})\geq\alpha_{k}^{3,j}
(recall that
\overline{D_{k}^{j}}\cap\Sigma=\emptyset
) we get

\mu_{A_{k},u_{k}}(\overline{D_{k}^{j}})-\mu_{B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta}}(\overline{D_{k}^{j}})\geq-c_{3}^{*}\sqrt{\delta}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{j},\nu_{j}}(x_{j})\cap\partial^{*}F^{h_{j}})

for some
c_{3}^{*}
depending only on

and
b_{1},b_{2}.

Summing these inequalities in
