we get

(5.40)

\displaystyle I_{3}\geq c_{3}^{*}\sqrt{\delta}\sum\limits_{h=0}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{h})

for all
k>k_{\delta}^{3}=\max_{j}k_{\delta}^{3,j}.

Including (
5.36
), (
5.38
) and (
5.40
) in (
5.34
) and using (
5.33
) in (
5.32
) we deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(A_{k},u_{k})-\mathcal{S}(B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta})\geq-c^{*}\sqrt{\delta}\Big(1+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{k}})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}A_{k})+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{i})\Big)

for all
k>k_{\delta}=\max\{k_{\delta}^{1},k_{\delta}^{2},k_{\delta}^{3}\}
.
Finally, since the elastic energy density is nonnegative, and invariant w.r.t. to additive piecewise rigid displacements

\mathcal{W}(A_{k},u_{k})\geq\mathcal{W}(B_{k}^{\delta},v_{k}^{\delta})

and hence, (
5.4
) follows.
∎

From Theorems
2.5
and
2.6
together with Proposition
A.1
implies that the minimum problem (
2.12
) is solvable.

Proof of Theorem
2.4
.

Fix any
\lambda>0
and let
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}\subset\mathcal{C}
be a minimizing sequence for
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}.
Then
\sup_{k}\mathcal{F}(A_{k},u_{k})>+\infty,
and hence, by Theorem
2.6
there exists a not relabelled subsequence
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\},
a sequence
\{(B_{k},v_{k})\}\subset\mathcal{C}
and
(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}
such that
(B_{k},v_{k})\overset{\tau_{\mathcal{C}}}{\to}(A,u),

|A_{k}\Delta B_{k}|\to 0
and

(5.41)

\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\,\mathcal{F}(A_{k},u_{k})\geq\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}(B_{k},v_{k})\geq\mathcal{F}(A,u).

Since the map
E\mapsto||E|-\mathtt{v}|
is
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-continuous, from (
5.41
) it follows that

\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\,\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}(A_{k},u_{k})\geq\liminf\limits_{k\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}(B_{k},v_{k})\geq\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}(B,v).

Hence,
(B,v)
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}.

By Proposition
A.1
there exists
\lambda_{0}>0
such that for
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
every minimizer
(A,u)
of
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}
satisfies the volume constraint
|A|=\mathtt{v}.
Thus,

(A,u)
solves also the problem (
2.12
).
Conversely, if
(A,u)
solves (
2.12
), then for
\lambda>\lambda_{0},

\displaystyle\min_{(B,v)\in\mathcal{C},\,|B|=\mathtt{v}}\mathcal{F}(B,v)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(A,u)=\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}(A,u)\geq\min_{(B,v)\in\mathcal{C}}\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}(B,v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{(B,v)\in\mathcal{C},\,|B|=\mathtt{v}}\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}(B,v)=\min_{(B,v)\in\mathcal{C},\,|B|=\mathtt{v}}\mathcal{F}(B,v)

and hence,
(A,u)
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}.

∎

5.2.
Compactness in
\mathcal{C}_{p}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\rm Dir}

In this section we comment on the
\tau
-compactness of energy-equibounded sequences in
\mathcal{C}_{p}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\rm Dir};
for the definition of
\tau
-convergence see (
2.18
). Using (
2.15
) and the compactness result
[
14
, Theorem 1.1]

we have:

–

if
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}\subset\mathcal{C}_{p}
is arbitrary sequence with
\sup_{k}\mathcal{F}_{p}(A_{k},u_{k})<+\infty,
then repeating the same arguments in the proof of Proposition
5.1
we construct a not relabelled subsequence, the set
G_{k}^{\delta},
numbers
s_{\delta}
and
k_{\delta}
satisfying (
5.1a
)-(
5.1d
) such that the configuration
(B_{k}^{\delta},v_{k}^{\delta})\in\mathcal{C}_{p},
given by (
5.2
) and (
5.3
), satisfies

\mathcal{S}(A_{k},u_{k})-\mathcal{S}(B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k}^{\delta})\geq-c^{*}\sqrt{\delta}\Big(1+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{k}})+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}A_{k})+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{m}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}F^{i})\Big).

Then by (
2.15
)

\mathcal{W}(A_{k},u_{k})\geq\mathcal{W}(B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k})+\int_{G_{k}^{\delta}}W_{p}(x,\mathcal{E}v_{k}^{\delta})\mathrm{d}x\geq\mathcal{W}(B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k})-\int_{G_{k}^{\delta}}|f|\mathrm{d}x.

Since
f\in L^{1}(\Omega\cup S),
by (
5.1c
) and the absolute continuity of the Lebesgue integral we have

(5.42)

\mathcal{W}(A_{k},u_{k})\geq\mathcal{W}(B_{k}^{\delta},u_{k})+o_{\delta},

where
o_{\delta}\to 0
as
\delta\to 0.
Now the proof of the compactness in
\mathcal{C}_{p}
runs exactly the same as Theorem
2.6
using (
5.42
) in place of (
5.6
);

–

if
\{(A_{k},u_{k})\}\subset\mathcal{C}_{p}
is arbitrary sequence with
\sup_{k}\mathcal{F}_{\rm Dir}(A_{k},u_{k})<+\infty,
then
by
[
14
, Theorem 1.1]
in the proof of Theorem
2.6
we will have only two sets
F^{0}
and
F^{1}
partitioning
A:
the sequence
u_{k}
converges a.e. in
F^{1}
(up to a subsequence) and
|u_{k}|\to+\infty
a.e. in
F^{0}.
In particular, due to the Dirichlet condition for
u_{k}
in
S,
we do not need to add any rigid displacements, and then the proofs runs as in
\mathcal{C}_{p}
.

The
\tau
-compactness in
\mathcal{C}_{p}
(resp.
\mathcal{C}_{\rm Dir}
) and the
\tau
-lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{F}_{p}
(resp.
\mathcal{F}_{\rm Dir}
) imply that for any
\lambda>0
there exists a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\lambda}
(resp.
\mathcal{F}_{\rm Dir}^{\lambda}
). Now obverving that the proof of Proposition
A.1
works also in
\mathcal{C}_{p}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\rm Dir}
(see Remark
A.2
) we conclude that both minimum problems (
2.16
) and (
2.17
) admit a solution.

6.
Decay estimates

This section is devoted to the proof of the following density estimates for minimizers of
\mathcal{F}
.

Theorem 6.1

(
Density estimates
)
.

There exist
\varsigma_{*}=\varsigma_{*}(b_{3},b_{4})\in(0,1)
and
R_{*}=R_{*}(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4})>0,
where
b_{i}
are given by (
2.8
) and (
2.10
), with the following property. Let
(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}
be any minimizer of
\mathcal{F}
in
\mathcal{C}
such that
\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A\subset_{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}}J_{u}
and
\int_{\Omega\setminus A}|\mathcal{E}u|\mathrm{d}x=0,
and let

(6.1)

J_{u}^{*}:=\{x\in J_{u}:\,\,\theta(J_{u},x)=1\}.

Then for any
x\in\Omega
and
r\in(0,\min\{1,\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)\})

(6.2)

\frac{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}(x)\cap J_{u})}{r^{n-1}}\leq\frac{4nb_{2}+\lambda_{0}}{b_{1}}.

Moreover, if
x\in\Omega\cap\overline{J_{u}^{*}}
and
r\in(0,R_{*})
with
Q_{r}(x)\subset\Omega,
then

(6.3)

\frac{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}(x)\cap J_{u})}{r^{n-1}}\geq\varsigma_{*}.

In particular,

(6.4)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\Omega\cap[\overline{J_{u}^{*}}\setminus J_{u}^{*}])=0.

Since
J_{u}
is
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-rectifiable, by the rectifiability criterion
[
3
, Theorem 2.63]

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u}\setminus J_{u}^{*})=0.
Thus, if we remove a
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-negligible set from
J_{u},
then (
6.4
) implies that the jump set of
u
is essentially closed in
\Omega
.

To prove Theorem
6.1
we follow the arguments of
[
37
, Section 3]
. First we introduce the local version
\mathcal{F}(\cdot;O):\mathcal{C}\to\mathbb{R}
of
\mathcal{F}
in open sets
O\subset\Omega
as

(6.5)

\mathcal{F}(A,u;O):=\mathcal{S}(A,u;O)+\mathcal{W}(A,u;O),

where
\mathcal{S}(\cdot;O)
and
\mathcal{W}(\cdot;O)
are the local versions of the surface and the elastic energy, i.e.,

\mathcal{S}(A,u;O):=\int_{O\cap\partial^{*}A}\varphi(y,\nu_{A})d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+2\int_{O\cap A^{(1)}\cap J_{u}}\varphi(y,\nu_{A})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

and

\mathcal{W}(A,u;O)=\int_{O\cap A}\mathbb{C}(y)\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\,\mathrm{d}y.

Next we introduce the notion of quasi-minimizers.

Definition 6.2

(
\Theta
-minimizers
)
.

Given
\Theta\geq 0,
the configuration
(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}

is a local
\Theta
-minimizer
of
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathbb{R}
in
O
if

\mathcal{F}(A,u;O)\leq\mathcal{F}(B,v;O)+\Theta|A\Delta B|

whenever
(B,v)\in\mathcal{C}
with
A\Delta B\subset\subset O
and
\mathrm{supp}\,(u-v)\subset\subset O.

For any
(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}
and any open set
O\subset\subset\Omega
let

(6.6)

\Phi(A,u;O):=\inf\Big\{\mathcal{F}(B,v;O):\,\,(B,v)\in\mathcal{C},B\Delta A\subset\subset O,\,\,\mathrm{supp}\,(u-v)\subset\subset O\Big\},

and let

(6.7)

\Psi(A,u;O):=\mathcal{F}(A,u;O)-\Phi(A,u;O)

be the
deviation of
(A,u)
from minimality
in
O.

The following proposition is a generalization to our setting of

[
12
, Theorem 4]
established for the Griffith model.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
Q_{R}(x_{0})\subset\subset\Omega.
Consider sequences of Finsler norms
\{\varphi_{h}\}
and ellipticity tensors
\{\mathbb{C}_{h}\}
such that
\{\mathbb{C}_{h}\}
is equicontinuous in
\overline{Q_{R}(x_{0})}
and there exist
d_{3},d_{4},d_{5}>0
with

(6.8)

d_{3}M:M\leq\mathbb{C}_{h}(x)M:M\leq d_{4}M:M\quad\text{for all $(x,M)\in\overline{Q_{R}(x_{0})}\times\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}_{\rm sym}$}

and

(6.9)

\inf\limits_{(x,\nu)\in\overline{Q_{R}(x_{0})}\times\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\,\phi_{h}(x,\nu)\geq d_{5}\sup\limits_{(x,\nu)\in\overline{Q_{R}(x_{0})}\times\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\,\phi_{h}(x,\nu),

and define

\mathcal{F}_{h}
and
\Psi_{h}
in
\mathcal{C}
as in (
6.5
) and (
6.7
), respectively, with
\varphi_{h}
and
\mathbb{C}_{h}
in places of
\varphi
and
\mathbb{C}.

Let
\{(A_{h},u_{h})\}\subset\mathcal{C}
be such that

(6.10a)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{R}(x_{0})\setminus A_{h}}|\mathcal{E}u_{h}|\mathrm{d}x=0,

(6.10b)

\displaystyle M:=\sup\limits_{h\geq 1}\mathcal{F}_{h}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{R}(x_{0}))<\infty,

(6.10c)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}\Psi_{h}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{R}(x_{0}))=0,

(6.10d)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{R}(x_{0})\cap J_{u_{h}})=0,

(6.10e)

\displaystyle Q_{R}(x_{0})\cap\partial^{*}A_{h}\subset_{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}}J_{u_{h}}.

Then there exist
u\in H^{1}(Q_{R}(x_{0})),
an elasticity tensor
\mathbb{C}\in C^{0}(\overline{Q_{R}(x_{0})};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}_{\rm sym})
and sequences
\{a_{j}\}
of rigid displacements and subsequences
\{(A_{h_{j}},u_{h_{j}})\}
,
\{\varphi_{h_{j}}\}
and
\{\mathbb{C}_{h_{j}}\}
such that

(i)

\mathbb{C}_{h_{j}}\to\mathbb{C}
uniformly in
\overline{Q_{R}(x_{0})}

and

w_{j}:=u_{h_{j}}-a_{j}\to u\,\,\text{a.e.\ in $Q_{R}(x_{0})$}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{E}w_{j}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{E}u\,\,\text{in $L^{2}(Q_{R}(x_{0}))$}

as
j\to\infty;

(ii)

for all
v\in u+H_{0}^{1}(Q_{R}(x_{0}))

(6.11)

\int_{Q_{R}(x_{0})}\mathbb{C}(y)\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\int_{Q_{R}(x_{0})}\mathbb{C}(y)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\,\mathrm{d}y;

(iii)

for any
r\in(0,R]

(6.12)

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{F}_{h}(A_{h_{j}},u_{h_{j}};Q_{r}(x_{0}))=\int_{Q_{r}(x_{0})}\mathbb{C}(x)\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\,\mathrm{d}x.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
R=1
and
x_{0}=0.

Also by (
6.10d
) we may assume
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap J_{u_{h}})<1/4
for any
h.

Let

b_{h}^{\prime}:=\inf\limits_{(x,\nu)\in\overline{Q_{R}(x:0)}\times\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\,\phi_{h}(x,\nu),\qquad b_{h}^{\prime\prime}:=\sup\limits_{(x,\nu)\in\overline{Q_{R}(x:0)}\times\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\,\phi_{h}(x,\nu)

so that by (
6.9
)

(6.13)

d_{5}b_{h}^{\prime\prime}\leq b_{h}^{\prime}\leq b_{h}^{\prime\prime}\quad\text{for any $h.$}

By
[
11
, Proposition 2]
and (
6.8
), there exist a constant
c_{o}
(depending only on
n
and
d_{3}
) and sequences
\{\omega_{h}\}
of a measurable subsets of
Q_{1}
with
|\omega_{h}|\leq c_{o}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap J_{u_{h}})
and
\{a_{h}\}
of rigid displacements such that

(6.14)

\int_{Q_{1}\setminus\omega_{h}}|u_{h}-a_{h}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq c_{o}\int_{Q_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{h}(x)\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\,\mathrm{d}x.

By (
6.10a
) and (
6.10b
),
\|(u_{h}-a_{h})\chi_{Q_{1}\setminus\omega_{h}}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{1})}\leq Mc_{o},
and thus, there exist
u\in L^{2}(Q_{1})
and a not relablled subsequence such that

(u_{h}-a_{h})\chi_{Q_{1}\setminus\omega_{h}}\rightharpoonup\widetilde{u}

in
L^{2}(Q_{1}).
Since
|\omega_{h}|\to 0,
the set

F:=\{y\in Q_{1}:\,\,\limsup\limits_{h\to\infty}|u_{h}(y)-a_{h}(y)|=+\infty\}

satisfies
|F|=0.
Furthermore, by (
6.10a
), (
6.8
) and (
6.10b
) as well as the equality
J_{u_{h}}=J_{u_{h}-a_{h}}

\sup\limits_{h\geq 1}\int_{Q_{1}}|\mathcal{E}(u_{h}-a_{h})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap J_{u_{h}-a_{h}})<\frac{M}{d_{3}}+\frac{1}{4},

and hence, by
[
14
, Theorem 1.1]
there exist a not relabelled subsequence
\{u_{h}-a_{h}\}

and
u\in GSBD^{2}(Q_{1})
such that

(6.15)

\displaystyle u_{h}-a_{h}\to u\qquad\text{a.e.\ in $Q_{1}$}

(6.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(u_{h}-a_{h})\rightharpoonup\mathcal{E}u\qquad\text{in $L^{2}(Q_{1};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}_{\rm sym}),$}

(6.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap J_{u})\leq\liminf\limits_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{h}})=0.

Since the weak limit and the pointwise limit coincide (see e.g.,
[
22
, page 266]
),
\widetilde{u}=u
a.e. in
Q_{1}.
Moreover, (
6.14
), (
6.15
) and the Fatou’s Lemma imply
u\in L^{2}(Q_{1})
and by (
6.17
) one has
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u})=0.
Thus, by Lemma
A.4

u\in H^{1}(Q_{1})
.
Since our elastic energy is invariant under additive rigid displacements, without loss of generality further we assume
a_{h}=0
for any
h\geq 1.

Next we prove (
6.11
). Let
v\in H^{1}(Q_{1})
be such that
\mathrm{supp}\,(u-v)\subset\subset Q_{r}
for some
r\in(0,1).
Fix
r^{\prime\prime}<r^{\prime}<r
and let
\psi\in C_{c}^{1}(Q_{r};[0,1])
be a cut-off function with

\{0<\psi<1\}\subset\{u=v\}\cap Q_{r^{\prime}}
and
\mathrm{supp}\,(u-v)\subseteq\{\psi\equiv 1\}\subseteq Q_{r^{\prime\prime}}.

By (
6.10d
) and
[
12
, Theorem 3]
there exist a positive constant
c>0
(depending only on
n,

d_{3}
and
d_{4}
), a function
\widetilde{v}_{h}\in GSBD^{2}(Q_{1}),

r_{h}\in(r-\delta_{h},r)
with

(6.18)

\delta_{h}:=\sqrt[2n]{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{h}})},

and a Lebesgue measurable set
\widetilde{\omega}_{h}\subset Q_{r_{h}}
such that

(
\rm a_{1}
)

\widetilde{v}_{h}\in C^{\infty}(Q_{r-\delta_{h}}),

\widetilde{v}_{h}=u_{h}
in
Q_{1}\setminus Q_{r_{h}},
and

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{h}}\cap\partial Q_{r_{h}})=\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{\widetilde{v}_{h}}\cap\partial Q_{r_{h}})=0;

(
\rm a_{2}
)

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{\widetilde{v}_{h}}\setminus J_{u_{h}})<c\delta_{h}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{h}}\cap(Q_{r}\setminus Q_{r-\delta_{h}}));

(
\rm a_{3}
)

|\widetilde{\omega}_{h}|\leq c\delta_{h}^{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r_{h}}\cap J_{u_{h}})
and by (
6.8
),

(6.19)

\int_{Q_{r}\setminus\widetilde{\omega}_{h}}|\widetilde{v}_{h}-u_{h}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\leq c\delta_{h}^{4}\int_{Q_{r}}\mathbb{C}_{h}(x)\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x;

(
\rm a_{4}
)

if
\eta\in\mathrm{Lip}(Q_{1};[0,1]),
then

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r}}\eta\mathbb{C}_{h}(x)\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}\mathrm{d}x\leq\int_{Q_{r}}\eta\mathbb{C}_{h}(x)\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x

(6.20)

\displaystyle+c\delta_{h}^{s}[1+\mathrm{Lip}(\eta)]\int_{Q_{r}}\mathbb{C}_{h}(x)\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x

for some
s\in(0,1)
independent of
h.

By (
\rm a_{1}
)
\widetilde{v}_{h}\in H^{1}(Q_{r^{\prime}})
and
\mathrm{supp}\,(\widetilde{v}_{h}-u_{h})\subset\subset Q_{r}
for all sufficiently large
h.

By (
6.15
), (
6.19
) and the relation
\delta_{h}^{2n}=\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap J_{u_{h}})\to 0
it follows that

\widetilde{v}_{h}\to u
a.e. in
Q_{1}.
Define

(6.21)

v_{h}:=(1-\psi)\widetilde{v}_{h}+\psi v.

Then
(A_{h},v_{h})
is an admissible configuration for
\Phi_{h}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{1})
in (
6.6
). Therefore from (
6.10c
) and the definition of deviation it follows that

(6.22)

\mathcal{F}_{h}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{1})\leq\mathcal{F}_{h}(A_{h},v_{h};Q_{1})+o(1),

where
o(1)\to 0
as
h\to\infty.
Note that
by (
\rm a_{1}
), (
\rm a_{2}
), (
6.13
) and (
6.10b
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(A_{h},v_{h};Q_{1})-\mathcal{S}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{1})=

\displaystyle\int_{A_{h}^{(1)}\cap J_{\widetilde{v}_{h}}}\phi_{h}(x,\nu_{J_{\widetilde{v}_{h}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-\int_{A_{h}^{(1)}\cap J_{u_{h}}}\phi_{h}(x,\nu_{J_{u_{h}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{A_{h}^{(1)}\cap(J_{\widetilde{v}_{h}}\setminus J_{u_{h}})\cap(Q_{r}\setminus Q_{r-\delta_{h}})}\phi_{h}(x,\nu_{J_{\widetilde{v}_{h}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle b_{h}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{\widetilde{v}_{h}}\setminus J_{u_{h}})\leq cb_{h}^{\prime\prime}\delta_{h}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u_{h}}\cap(Q_{r}\setminus Q_{r-\delta_{h}}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c\delta_{h}}{d_{5}}\mathcal{S}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{1})\leq\frac{Mc\delta_{h}}{d_{5}}.

This estimate, (
6.22
) and the definition of localized elastic energy imply

(6.23)

\int_{A_{h}\cap Q_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{h}(x)\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq\int_{A_{h}\cap Q_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{h}(x)\mathcal{E}v_{h}:\mathcal{E}v_{h}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+o(1)

as
h\to+\infty.

Next we estimate the integral in the right-hand-side of (
6.23
). By (
6.21
)

\mathcal{E}v_{h}=(1-\psi)\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}+\psi\mathcal{E}v+\nabla\psi\odot(v-\widetilde{v}_{h}),

where
X\odot Y=(X\otimes Y+Y\otimes X)/2.
Since
\widetilde{v}_{h}\to u
a.e. in
Q_{r}
and
u=v
in
Q_{r}\setminus Q_{r^{\prime}},
one has
v_{h}\to v
a.e. in
Q_{1}.

We claim that
\widetilde{v}_{h}\to u
strongly in
L_{\mathrm{loc}}^{2}(Q_{r}).
Indeed, fix any
\rho\in(0,r).
By (
\rm a_{1}
)
\widetilde{v}_{h}\in H^{1}(Q_{\rho}).
By (
6.8
), (
6.10b
) and (
(

a
4

)
) (applied with
\eta=1
)

d_{3}\int_{Q_{\rho}}|\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\leq d_{3}\int_{Q_{r}}|\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{Q_{r}}\mathbb{C}_{h}(x)\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x\leq MC

for some constant
C>0
independent of
h.
Moreover, by the Poincaré-Korn inequality for each
h
there exist a rigid displacement
e_{h}
(possibly depending also on
\rho
) such that

\|\widetilde{v}_{h}-e_{h}\|_{H^{1}(Q_{\rho})}\leq\int_{Q_{\rho}}|\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{MCC^{\prime}}{d_{3}}

and hence, the Rellich-Kondrachov Theorem implies the existence of
w\in H^{1}(Q_{\rho})
and not relabelled subsequence such that

\widetilde{v}_{h}-e_{h}\to w
in
L^{2}(Q_{\rho}).
Since
\widetilde{v}_{h}\to u
a.e. in
Q_{1},

e_{h}\to w-u
and hence,
e:=u-w
is also a rigid displacement. Then

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{h\to\infty}\|\widetilde{v}_{h}-u\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\rho})}\leq\limsup\limits_{h\to\infty}\|\widetilde{v}_{h}-e_{h}-w\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\rho})}+\limsup\limits_{h\to\infty}\|e_{h}+(w-u)\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\rho})}=0,

and the claim follows.

Since
u=v
out of
\{\psi=1\},
the claim implies

\widetilde{v}_{h}\to v
strongly in
L^{2}(\{0<\psi<1\}),
and hence,

(6.24)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}\int_{Q_{r}}|\nabla\psi\odot(v-\widetilde{v}_{h})\big|_{A_{h}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\leq

\displaystyle\liminf\limits_{h\to\infty}\int_{\{0<\psi<1\}}|\nabla\psi\odot(v-\widetilde{v}_{h})|^{2}\mathrm{d}x=0.

Thus, by definition (
6.21
) of
v_{h}

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r}\cap A_{h}}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}v_{h}:\mathcal{E}v_{h}

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}x=\int_{Q_{r}\cap A_{h}}(1-\psi)^{2}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}:\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{Q_{r}\cap A_{h}}\psi^{2}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{Q_{r}\cap A_{h}}\mathbb{C}_{h}(\nabla\psi\odot(v-\widetilde{v}_{h})):(\nabla\psi\odot(v-\widetilde{v}_{h}))\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{Q_{r}\cap A_{h}}(1-\psi)\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}:(\nabla\psi\odot(v-\widetilde{v}_{h}))\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{Q_{r}}\psi\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}v:(\nabla\psi\odot(v-\widetilde{v}_{h}))\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r}\cap A_{h}}(1-\psi)^{2}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}:\mathcal{E}\widetilde{v}_{h}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{Q_{r}\cap A_{h}}\psi^{2}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x+o(1)

(6.25)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r}\cap A_{h}}(1-\psi)^{2}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{Q_{r}\cap A_{h}}\psi^{2}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x+o(1),

where in the second equality we use (
6.10b
), (
(

a
4

)
) with
\eta\equiv 1,
(
6.24
), (
6.8
) and the Hölder inequality, while in the last inequality we use (
(

a
4

)
) with
\eta=(1-\psi)^{2}
and (
6.10d
).
Now combining (
6
) with (
6.23
) we get

(6.26)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r}}(2\psi-\psi^{2})\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x\leq\int_{Q_{r}}\psi^{2}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x+o(1).

Since
\{\mathbb{C}_{h}\}
is equibounded (see (
6.8
)) and equicontinuous, by the Arzela-Ascoli Theorem, there exist a (not relabelled) subsequence and an elasticity tensor
\mathbb{C}\in C^{0}(Q_{1};\mathbb{M}^{n\times n}_{\rm sym})
such that
\mathbb{C}_{h}\to\mathbb{C}
uniformly in
Q_{1}.
Hence,
letting
h\to\infty
in (
6.26
) and using (
6.16
) and the convexity of the elastic energy, we obtain

(6.27)

\int_{Q_{r}}(2\psi-\psi^{2})\mathbb{C}(y)\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\int_{Q_{r}}\psi^{2}\mathbb{C}(y)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\,\mathrm{d}y.

By the choice of
\psi,
(
6.27
) implies

(6.28)

\int_{Q_{r^{\prime\prime}}}\mathbb{C}(y)\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\int_{Q_{r}}\mathbb{C}(y)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\,\mathrm{d}y.

Since
r^{\prime\prime}
is arbitrary, letting
r^{\prime\prime}\nearrow r
we deduce that (
6.28
) holds also with
r^{\prime\prime}=r.
Since
\mathrm{supp}\,(u-v)\subset\subset Q_{r}
, this implies (
6.11
).

It remains to prove (
6.12
).
If we take
v=u
in (
6.26
) and use
0\leq\psi\leq 1
and
\psi=1
in
Q_{r^{\prime\prime}}

we get

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{r^{\prime\prime}}}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}udx\leq

\displaystyle\liminf\limits_{h\to\infty}\int_{Q_{r^{\prime\prime}}}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{h\to\infty}\int_{Q_{r^{\prime\prime}}}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x\leq\int_{Q_{r}}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\mathrm{d}x.

Since
r^{\prime\prime}
is arbitrary, letting
r^{\prime\prime}\nearrow r
we deduce

(6.29)

\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}\int_{Q_{r}}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x=\int_{Q_{r}}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\mathrm{d}x.

In view of (
6.29
) to prove (
6.12
) it suffices to establish

(6.30)

\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}\mathcal{S}_{h}(A_{h};Q_{r})=0

for any
r\in(0,1).
By (
6.10e
)
Q_{1}\cap\partial^{*}A_{h}\subset J_{u_{h}}
up to an
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
-negligible set. Thus, by (
6.10d
) and the relative isoperimetric inequality, up to a subsequence, either

(6.31)

\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}|Q_{1}\cap A_{h}|=0

or

(6.32)

\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}|Q_{1}\setminus A_{h}|=0.

We claim that there exists a not relabelled subsequence
\{A_{h}\}
such that for a.e.
t\in(0,1)

(6.33)

\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}\int_{A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t}}\phi_{h}(x,\nu_{Q_{t}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=0

if (
6.31
) holds, and

(6.34)

\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}\int_{(Q_{1}\setminus A_{h})\cap\partial Q_{t}}\phi_{h}(x,\nu_{Q_{t}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=0

if (
6.32
) holds.

We establish only (
6.31
), the proof of (
6.34
) being similar. By the coarea formula (applied with
f(x)=\max\{|x_{1}|,\ldots,|x_{n}|\}
)

\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}|Q_{1}\cap A_{h}|=\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t})\mathrm{d}t=0,

thus, passing to further not relabelled subsequence,
\lim\limits_{h\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t})=0
for a.e.
t\in(0,1).
In particular, if
\sup_{h}b_{h}^{\prime\prime}<+\infty,
then

(6.35)

\limsup\limits_{h\to+\infty}\int_{A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t}}\phi_{h}(x,\nu_{Q_{t}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq\limsup\limits_{h\to+\infty}\,\,b_{h}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t})=0.

On the other hand, if
b_{h}^{\prime\prime}\to+\infty
(up to a subsequence), then by the coarea formula and the relative isoperimetric inequality in
Q_{1}

(6.36)

b_{h}^{\prime\prime}\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t})\mathrm{d}t=b_{h}^{\prime\prime}|A_{h}\cap Q_{1}|\leq b_{h}^{\prime\prime}c_{n}P(A_{h},Q_{1})^{\frac{n}{n-1}},

where
c_{n}>0
is the relative isoperimetric inequality for cubes.
By (
6.9
)

P(A_{h},Q_{1})\leq\frac{1}{a_{h}}\,\mathcal{S}(A_{h},Q_{1})\leq\frac{1}{d_{5}b_{h}^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathcal{S}(A_{h},Q_{1})\leq\frac{\mathcal{F}_{h}(A_{h},u_{h},Q_{1})}{d_{5}b_{h}^{\prime\prime}},

hence, by (
6.36
)

b_{h}^{\prime\prime}\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t})\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{n}\,\Big[\frac{M}{d_{5}}\Big]^{\frac{n}{n-1}}\,[b_{h}^{\prime\prime}]^{-\frac{1}{n-1}}.

This and (
6.10b
) imply

\lim\limits_{h\to+\infty}b_{h}^{\prime\prime}\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t})\mathrm{d}t=0.

In particular,

\lim\limits_{h\to+\infty}\,b_{h}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t})=0

for a.e.
t\in(0,1).
Now the proof of (
6.33
) follows as in (
6.35
).

Now we prove (
6.30
) assuming (
6.31
). Given
t\in(r,1)
for which (
6.33
) holds, define
E_{h}:=A_{h}\setminus Q_{t}.
Then
(E_{h},u_{h})
is an admissible configuration in (
6.6
), and
thus,

(6.37)

\mathcal{F}_{h}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{1})\leq\Phi_{h}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{1})+o(1)\leq\mathcal{F}_{h}(E_{h},u_{h};Q_{1})+o(1),

where in the first inequality we use (
6.10c
) and in the second we use the definition of
\Phi_{h}.

From the definition of
E_{h}
and (
6.37
) it follows that

\mathcal{S}_{h}(A_{h};Q_{t})\leq\int_{A_{h}\cap\partial Q_{t}}\phi_{h}(x,\nu_{Q_{t}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+o(1).

This and (
6.33
) imply (
6.30
).

Now suppose that (
6.32
) holds. Let
\delta_{h}
be defined as in (
6.18
), and let and
\psi,
and
r^{\prime\prime}<r^{\prime}<r
and
v_{h}
be as in (
6.21
) with
v=u
.
Fix any
t\in(r,1)
for which (
6.34
) holds and set
E_{h}:=A_{h}\cup\overline{Q_{t}}.
Then for sufficiently large
h
that
(E_{h},v_{h})
is an admissible configuration for
\Phi_{h}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{1})
in (
6.6
). Thus by (
6.10c
)

\mathcal{F}_{h}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{1})\leq\mathcal{F}_{h}(E_{h},v_{h};Q_{1})+o(1).

By the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{h}
, as in the proof of (
6.26
) we establish

\mathcal{S}_{h}(A_{h};Q_{t})+\int_{Q_{r}}(2\psi-\psi^{2})\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}u_{h}:\mathcal{E}u_{h}\mathrm{d}x\\
\leq\int_{Q_{r}}\psi^{2}\mathbb{C}_{h}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\mathrm{d}x+\int_{(Q_{1}\setminus A_{h})\cap\partial Q_{t}}\phi_{h}(x,\nu_{Q_{t}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+o(1).

Thus, as in (
6.27
) letting
h\to\infty
we obtain

(6.38)

\displaystyle\limsup\limits_{h\to\infty}\mathcal{S}_{h}(E_{h};Q_{t})+\int_{Q_{r}}(2\psi-\psi^{2})\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}udx\leq\int_{Q_{r}}\psi^{2}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}udx.

Since
\psi=1
in
Q_{r^{\prime\prime}}
and
|\psi|\leq 1,
from (
6.38
) it follows that

\limsup\limits_{h\to\infty}\mathcal{S}_{h}(A_{h};Q_{t})+\int_{Q_{r^{\prime\prime}}}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}udx\leq\int_{Q_{r}}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}udx.

Now letting
r^{\prime\prime}\to r
we get (
6.30
).
∎

Recall that by
[
42
, Theorem 6.2.1]

if the elasticity tensor
\mathbb{C}
is constant and satisfies (
2.10
), then there exists
C_{b_{3},b_{4}}>0
such that every local minimizer
u\in H^{1}(Q_{1}(x_{0}))
of the functional

(6.39)

v\in H^{1}(Q_{1}(x_{0});\mathbb{R}^{n})\mapsto\int_{Q_{1}(x_{0})}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x

is analytic in
Q_{1}(x_{0})
and satisfies

(6.40)

\int_{Q_{r}(x_{0})}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C_{b_{3},b_{4}}\,r^{n}\int_{Q_{1}(x)}\mathbb{C}\mathcal{E}u:\mathcal{E}u\,\mathrm{d}x

for any
r\in(0,1/2).
Let

(6.41)

\tau_{0}:=\tau_{0}(b_{3},b_{4}):=(1+C_{b_{3},b_{4}})^{-2}.

Using Proposition
6.3
and repeating similar arguments in
[
13
]
we get the following decay property of the functional
\mathcal{F}
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Assume (H1)-(H3). For any
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
there exist

\varsigma=\varsigma(\tau)\in(0,1),

\vartheta:=\vartheta(\tau)>0
and
R:=R(\tau)>0
such that if
(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}
satisfies

\displaystyle Q_{\rho}(x)\cap\partial^{*}A\subseteq J_{u},

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\rho}(x)\setminus A}|\mathcal{E}u|\mathrm{d}x=0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{\rho}(x)\cap J_{u})<\varsigma\rho^{n-1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\rho}(x))\leq(1+\vartheta)\Phi(A,u;Q_{\rho}(x))

for some
Q_{\rho}(x)\subset\subset\Omega
with
0<\rho<R,
then

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\tau\rho}(x))\leq\tau^{n-1/2}\,\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\rho}(x)).

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that there exist
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
, positive real numbers
\varsigma_{h},\vartheta_{h},\rho_{h}\to 0,
cubes
Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h})\subset\subset\Omega
, and admissible configurations
(A_{h},u_{h})\in\mathcal{C}
such that

(6.42a)

\displaystyle Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h})\cap\partial^{*}A_{h}\subseteq J_{u_{h}},

(6.42b)

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h})\setminus A_{h}}|\mathcal{E}u_{h}|\mathrm{d}x=0,

(6.42c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h})\cap J_{u_{h}})\leq\varsigma_{h}\rho_{h}^{n-1},

(6.42d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h}))\leq(1+\vartheta_{h})\Phi(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h})),

but

(6.43)

\mathcal{F}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{\tau\rho_{h}}(x_{h}))>\tau^{n-1/2}\mathcal{F}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h}))

for any
h.
Note that
\mathcal{F}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h}))>0.
Let us define the rescaled energy
\mathcal{F}_{h}(\cdot;Q_{1})
as in (
6.5
) with

\phi_{h}(y,\nu):=\frac{(\rho_{h}/2)^{n-1}\varphi(x_{h}+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{h}y,\nu)}{\mathcal{F}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h}))}

in place of
\varphi(y,\nu)
and

\mathbb{C}_{h}(y):=\mathbb{C}(x_{h}+\rho_{h}y)

in place of
\mathbb{C}(y)
, for
y\in Q_{1}
. In view of (
6.42a
)-(
6.42d
) for

E_{h}:=\sigma_{x_{h},\rho_{h}}(A_{h})

(see definition of blow-up map
\sigma_{x,r}
at (
2.1
)) and

v_{h}(y):=\frac{(\rho_{h}/2)^{\frac{n-2}{2}}\,u_{h}(x_{h}+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{h}y)}{\sqrt{\mathcal{F}(A_{h},u_{h};Q_{\rho_{h}}(x_{h}))}}

we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}(E_{h},v_{h};Q_{1})=1,

\displaystyle Q_{1}\cap\partial^{*}E_{h}\subset_{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}}J_{v_{h}},

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{1}\setminus E_{h}}|\mathcal{E}v_{h}|\mathrm{d}x=0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{1}\cap\partial J_{v_{h}})<2^{n-1}\varsigma_{h},

\displaystyle\Psi_{h}(E_{h},v_{h};Q_{1})\leq\vartheta_{h}\Phi_{h}(E_{h},v_{h};Q_{1})\leq\vartheta_{h}\mathcal{F}_{h}(E_{h},v_{h};Q_{1})=\vartheta_{h},

where
\Phi_{h}
and
\Psi_{h}
are defined as in (
6.6
) and (
6.7
) (with
\varphi_{h}
and
\mathbb{C}_{h}
in places of
\varphi
and
\mathbb{C},
respectively). By the boundednes of
\Omega,
there exists
x_{0}\in\overline{\Omega}
such that, up to extracting a subsequence,
x_{h}\to x_{0}
as
h\to+\infty.
In particular,
x_{h}+\rho_{h}y\to x_{0}
for every
y\in\overline{Q_{1}}.
Then the uniform continuity of
\mathbb{C}
implies that
\mathbb{C}_{h}\to\mathbb{C}_{0}:=\mathbb{C}(x_{0})
uniformly in
\overline{Q_{1}}.

Also by (
2.8
)
\phi_{h}
satisfies (
6.9
) with
d_{5}:=b_{1}/b_{2}.
Thus, by Proposition
6.3
there exist
v\in H^{1}(Q_{1})
and infinitesimal rigid displacements
a_{h}
such that, up to a subsequence,

w_{h}:=v_{h}-a_{h}\to v

pointwise a.e. in
Q_{1}
,
\mathcal{E}w_{h}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{E}v
in
L^{2}(Q_{1})
as
h\to+\infty,

and

(6.44)

\lim\limits_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{h}(E_{h},v_{h};Q_{r})=\lim\limits_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}_{h}(E_{h},w_{h};Q_{r})=\int_{Q_{r}}\mathbb{C}_{0}(x)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x

for any
r\in(0,1].

In particular,
from (
6.43
) and (
6.44
) it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{\tau}}\mathbb{C}_{0}(x)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}(E_{h},v_{h};Q_{\tau})

\displaystyle\geq\tau^{n-1/2}\lim\limits_{h\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}(E_{h},v_{h};Q_{1})

\displaystyle=\tau^{n-1/2}\int_{Q_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{0}(x)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x.

Since
\mathcal{F}_{h}(E_{h},v_{h};Q_{1})=1,
by (
6.44
)
\int_{Q_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{0}(x)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}vdx=1.
Moreover, as
\mathbb{C}_{0}
is constant and
v
is a local minimizer of (
6.39
), applying (
6.40
) with
r:=\tau
and
R:=1
we get

\displaystyle C_{b_{3},b_{4}}\,\tau^{n}=

\displaystyle C_{b_{3},b_{4}}\,\tau^{n}\int_{Q_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{0}(x)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x\geq\int_{Q_{\tau}}\mathbb{C}_{0}(x)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tau^{n-1/2}\int_{Q_{1}}\mathbb{C}_{0}(x)\mathcal{E}v:\mathcal{E}v\mathrm{d}x=\tau^{n-1/2},

which contradicts to the assumption
\tau<\tau_{0}.

∎

By employing the arguments of
[
43
, Section 4.3]
and using Proposition
6.4

we establish the following lower bound for
\mathcal{F}
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Given
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
, let

\varsigma:=\varsigma(\tau)\in(0,1),

\vartheta=\vartheta(\tau)>0
and
R:=R(\tau)>0

be as in Proposition
6.4
and let

R_{0}:=R_{0}(\Theta,\tau,b_{1}):=\min\left\{R(\tau),\frac{b_{1}n\omega_{n}^{1/n}\vartheta}{\Theta(2+\vartheta)}\right\},\quad\Theta>0.

Let
(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}
be a
\Theta
-minimizer of
\mathcal{F}
in
Q_{r_{0}}(x_{0})
such that

\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A\subset_{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}}J_{u}
and
\int_{\Omega\setminus A}|\mathcal{E}u|\mathrm{d}x=0.
Then for any
x\in Q_{r_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\overline{J_{u}^{*}},
where
J_{u}^{*}
is given by (
6.1
), and any cube
Q_{\rho}(x)\subset Q_{r_{0}}(x_{0})
with
\rho\in(0,R_{0})
one has

(6.45)

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\rho}(x))\geq b_{1}\varsigma\rho^{n-1}.

Proof.

Let
(C,w),(D,v)\in\mathcal{C}
and
O\subset\Omega
be such that
C\Delta D\subset\subset O.

By the isoperimetric inequality, the inclusion
\partial^{*}(C\Delta D)\subset O\cap(\partial^{*}C\cup\partial^{*}D),
(
2.8
), the definition of
\mathcal{S}(\cdot;O)
and the nonnegativity of
\mathcal{W}(\cdot;O)
one has

\displaystyle n\omega_{n}^{1/n}\,|C\Delta D|^{\frac{n-1}{n}}\leq

\displaystyle P(C\Delta D)\leq P(C,O)+P(D,O)

(6.46)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{S}(C,w,O)+\mathcal{S}(D,v,O)}{b_{1}}\leq\frac{\mathcal{F}(C,w;O)+\mathcal{F}(D,v;O)}{b_{1}},

From (
6
) and the
\Theta
-minimality of
(A,u)
in
Q_{r_{0}}(x_{0})
we deduce

\displaystyle\!\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(B,v;Q_{r}(x))+\Theta|A\Delta B|^{\frac{1}{n}}|A\Delta B|^{\frac{n-1}{n}}

(6.47)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(B,v;Q_{r}(x))+\frac{\Theta r}{b_{1}n\omega_{n}^{1/n}}\Big(\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))+\mathcal{F}(B,v;Q_{r}(x))\Big)

for any
Q_{r}(x)\subset Q_{r_{0}}(x_{0})
and
(B,v)\in\mathcal{C}
with
A\Delta B\subset\subset Q_{r}(x)
and
\mathrm{supp}\,(u-v)\subset\subset Q_{r}(x)
, where in the last inequality we used the inequality
|A\Delta B|\leq|Q_{r}|=r^{n}.

By the choice of
R_{0},
if
r\in(0,R_{0}),
then
\frac{\Theta r}{b_{1}n\omega_{n}^{1/n}}\leq\frac{\vartheta}{2+\vartheta},
and thus, by (
6
)

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))\leq(1+\vartheta)\mathcal{F}(B,v;Q_{r}(x)).

By the arbitrariness of
(B,v)
this inequality is equivalent to

(6.48)

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))\leq(1+\vartheta)\Phi(A,u;Q_{r}(x)).

Now we prove (
6.45
). Fix any
x\in J_{u}^{*};
for simplicity we suppose that
x=0.
By contradiction, assume that

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\rho})<b_{1}\varsigma\rho^{n-1}

for some
Q_{\rho}\subset\subset Q_{r_{0}}(x_{0})
with
\rho\in(0,R_{0}).

Then by the nonnegativity of the elastic energy and
(
2.8
) one has

b_{1}\varsigma\rho^{n-1}>\mathcal{S}(A,u;Q_{\rho})\geq b_{1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{\rho}\cap J_{u})

so that

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{\rho}\cap J_{u})<\varsigma\rho^{n-1}.

By Proposition
6.4
and the definition (
6.41
) of
\tau_{0}

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\tau\rho})\leq\tau^{n-1/2}\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\rho})<b_{1}\varsigma(\tau\rho)^{n-1}

so that

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{\tau\rho}\cap J_{u})<\varsigma(\tau\rho)^{n-1}.

Then by induction,

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{\tau^{m}\rho}\cap J_{u})<\varsigma(\tau^{m}\rho)^{n-1}\quad\text{for any $m\geq 1.$}

However, by the definition of
J_{u}^{*}

1=\lim\limits_{m\to+\infty}\frac{\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{\tau^{m}\rho}\cap J_{u})}{(\tau^{m}\rho)^{n-1}}\leq\frac{2b_{1}\varsigma}{2b_{1}}=\varsigma<1,

a contradiction. Hence, (
6.45
) holds for any
x\in J_{u}^{*}.
Note that the map
\mathcal{F}(A,u;\cdot),
defined for open sets
O\subset\subset Q_{r_{0}}(x_{0})
extends to a positive Borel measure in
Q_{r_{0}}(x_{0}),
and therefore, by continuity of Borel measures, (
6.45
) extends also for
x\in Q_{r_{0}}(x_{0})\cap\overline{J_{u}^{*}}
.
∎

Now we are ready to prove (
6.2
) and (
6.3
).

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Let
(A,u)
be a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}
such that
\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A\subset J_{u}
and
\int_{\Omega\setminus A}|Eu|\mathrm{d}x=0
and let
\lambda_{0}>0
be given by Theorem
2.4
. Since
(A,u)
is also a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{0}},
for any open set
O\subset\Omega
and
(B,v)\in\mathcal{C}
with
A\Delta B\subset\subset O
and
\mathrm{supp}\,(u-v)\subset\subset O
we have

\mathcal{F}(A,u;O)\leq\mathcal{F}(B,v;O)+\lambda_{0}\big||A|-|B|\big|\leq\mathcal{F}(B,v;O)+\lambda_{0}\big|A\Delta B\big|.

Hence,
(A,u)
is
\lambda_{0}
-minimizer of
\mathcal{F}(\cdot;\Omega)
in
\Omega.

Let us prove (
6.2
). Fix
x\in\Omega
and let
r_{x}:=\min\{1,\mathrm{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)\}.
Then by the
\lambda_{0}
-minimality of
(A,u)

for any
r\in(0,r_{x})
and
\rho\in(r,r_{x})

(6.49)

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\rho}(x))\leq\mathcal{F}(A\setminus\overline{Q_{r}},u;Q_{\rho}(x))+\lambda_{0}|Q_{r}(x)\cap A|,

where for shortness
Q_{r}:=Q_{r}(x)
. Since
\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\rho}(x)\setminus\overline{Q_{r}(x)})=\mathcal{F}(A\setminus\overline{Q_{r}(x)},u;Q_{\rho}(x)\setminus\overline{Q_{r}(x)}),
from (
6.49
) and the definition and nonnegativity of
\mathcal{F}
we get

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))\leq\int_{\partial Q_{r}(x)}\varphi(x,\nu_{Q_{r}(x)})d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}+\lambda_{0}r^{n}.

By (
2.8
)

\int_{\partial Q_{r}(x)}\varphi(x,\nu_{Q_{r}(x)})d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq b_{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial Q_{r}(x))=2nb_{2}r^{n}

thus, using
r\leq 1
we obtain

(6.50)

\mathcal{F}(A,u;\overline{Q_{r}(x)})\leq(2nb_{2}+\lambda_{0})r^{n-1}.

Using the nonnegativity of
\mathcal{W}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))
, (
2.8
) and the equality
Q_{r}(x)\cap J_{u}=(Q_{r}(x)\cap\partial^{*}A)\cup(Q_{r}(x)\cap A^{(1)}\cap J_{u})
in (
6.50
) we get

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))\geq\mathcal{S}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))\geq b_{1}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}(x)\cap J_{u}).

Therefore,

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}(x)\cap J_{u})\leq\frac{2nb_{2}+\lambda_{0}}{b_{1}}\,r^{n-1}.

Next we prove (
6.3
). Fix
x\in\overline{J_{u}^{*}}.

For
\tau_{0}
, given by (
6.41
), let
\varsigma_{o}=\varsigma(\tau_{0}/2)\in(0,1)
and
R_{o}=R_{0}(\tau_{0}/2,b_{1},b_{2},\lambda_{0})>0
be as in Proposition
6.5
. By (
6.45
)

(6.51)

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\gamma r}(x))\geq b_{1}\varsigma_{o}(\gamma r)^{n-1}

for any
\gamma\in(0,1)
and
r\in(0,R_{o})
with
Q_{r}(x)\subset\Omega.
Let

\varsigma_{*}:=\varsigma(\tau_{*}),\quad\vartheta_{*}:=\vartheta(\tau^{*})\quad\text{and}\quad R_{*}:=\min\{R(\tau_{*}),R_{o}\}

be given by Proposition
6.4
for

(6.52)

2\tau_{*}:=\min\Big\{\frac{\tau_{0}}{2},\Big(\frac{b_{1}\varsigma_{o}}{2nb_{2}+\lambda_{0}}\Big)^{2}\Big\}

By contradiction, if
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(Q_{r}(x)\cap J_{u})<\varsigma_{*}r^{n-1},
then applying (
6.48
) with
\tau=\tau_{*}
we get

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))\leq(1+\vartheta_{*})\Phi(A,u;Q_{r}(x)).

Hence, by Proposition
6.4

\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{\tau_{*}r}(x))\leq\tau_{*}^{n-1/2}\mathcal{F}(A,u;Q_{r}(x))

so that by (
6.51
) and (
6.50
)

\tau_{*}^{1/2}\geq\frac{b_{1}\varsigma_{o}}{2nb_{2}+\lambda_{0}},

which contradicts to (
6.52
).

Finally, (
6.4
) follows from the density estimates together with a covering argument.
∎

From Theorem
6.1
we get the partial regularity of minimizers of
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof of Theorem
2.7
.

(i)-(iii). Let
(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{u})\in\mathcal{C}
be a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}
and let

A^{\prime}:=\widetilde{A}^{(1)},\qquad u^{\prime}:=\widetilde{u}\chi_{A^{\prime}\cup S}+\xi^{\prime}\chi_{\Omega\setminus A^{\prime}},

where
\xi^{\prime}\in(0,1)^{n}
is chosen such that
\Omega\cap\partial^{*}A^{\prime}\subset J_{u^{\prime}}.
By
[
41
, Chapter 15]
,
\partial A^{\prime}=\overline{\partial^{*}A^{\prime}}.
Clearly,
(A^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{F},
and by Theorem
6.1

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\overline{J_{u^{\prime}}^{*}}\setminus J_{u^{\prime}}^{*})=0.
Since
J_{u^{\prime}}
is rectifiable,
by
[
3
, Theorem 2.63]

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u^{\prime}}\setminus J_{u^{\prime}}^{*})=0
and hence observing
\Omega\cap\partial A^{\prime}=\Omega\cap\overline{\partial^{*}A^{\prime}}\subset\overline{J_{u^{\prime}}}
we observe

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(A^{\prime}\setminus\mathrm{Int}(A^{\prime}))\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial A^{\prime})\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial\Omega)+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u^{\prime}})<+\infty.

Now let

A:=\mathrm{Int}(A^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad u:=\widetilde{u}\chi_{A\cup S}+\xi^{\prime}\chi_{\Omega\setminus A}.

Since
|A\Delta A^{\prime}|\leq|\partial A^{\prime}|=0,

u=u^{\prime}
a.e. in
\Omega\cup S
and hence,
(A,u)
is also a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}.
Moreover,

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\widetilde{A}^{(1)}\setminus A)\leq\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial A^{\prime})<+\infty,\qquad\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u}\setminus J_{u}^{*})=\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u^{\prime}}\setminus J_{u^{\prime}}^{*})=0,

and

\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\overline{J_{u}^{*}}\setminus J_{u}^{*})=\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\overline{J_{u^{\prime}}^{*}}\setminus J_{u^{\prime}}^{*})=0.

Thus, (i) follows. The assertions (ii) and (iii) directly follow from the minimality of
(A,u)
and Theorem
6.1
.

(iv). Finally, if
E\subset A
is a connected component of (the open set)
A
with
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial^{*}E\cap\Sigma\setminus J_{u})=0,
then for
v:=u\chi_{A\cup S\setminus E}+u_{0}\chi_{E}
we have

\mathcal{S}(A,u)\geq\mathcal{S}(A,v)

and

(6.53)

\mathcal{W}(A,u)\geq\mathcal{W}(A,v).

In (
6.53
) the equality holds if and only of
u=u_{0}
in
E.
Therefore, by the minimality of
(A,u)
it follows that
u=u_{0}
in
E
(up to an additive rigid displacement). It remains to prove

|E|\geq\omega_{n}\,\Big(\frac{b_{1}n}{\lambda_{0}}\Big)^{n}.

Consider the competitor
(A\setminus E,u)\in\mathcal{C}.
Since
(A,u)
solves (
2.13
),
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{0}}(A,u)\leq\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{0}}(A\setminus E,u)
so that using
u=u_{0}
in
E
and the additivity of the surface energy, we get

\int_{\partial^{*}E}\varphi(x,\nu_{E})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq\lambda_{0}|E|.

Using (
2.8
) and the isoperimetric inequality in this estimate we obtain

\lambda_{0}|E|\geq b_{1}P(E)\geq b_{1}n\omega_{n}^{1/n}|E|^{\frac{n-1}{n}}.

Hence,
|E|\geq(b_{1}n\omega_{n}^{1/n}/\lambda_{0})^{n}
and (iv) follows.
∎

Appendix A

A.1.
Equivalence of volume-constrained and uncontrained penalized minimum problems

The following proposition can be seen an extension of
[
26
, Theorem 1.1]
.

Proposition A.1
.

Assume (H1)-(H3). There exists
\lambda_{0}>0
(possibly depending on
b_{1},

b_{2}
and
\Omega
) with the following property:
(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}
is a solution of (
2.12
) if and only if
(A,u)
is also a solution to (
2.13
) for all
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}.

Proof.

Note that any minimizer
(A,u)\in\mathcal{C}
of
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}
with
|A|=\mathtt{v}
is also minimizer of
\mathcal{F}.
Hence, it suffices to show that there exists
\lambda_{0}>0
such that any minimizer
(A,u)
of
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}
for
\lambda>\lambda_{0}
satisfies
|A|=\mathtt{v}.

Assume by contradiction that there exist a sequence
\lambda_{h}\to\infty
and a sequence
(A_{h},u_{h})\in\mathcal{C}
minimizing
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}
such that
|A_{h}|\neq\mathtt{v}.
Take any
A_{0}\in BV(\Omega;\{0,1\})
with
|A|=\mathtt{v}.
Then by minimality,
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(A_{h},u_{h})\leq\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(A_{0},u_{0})=\mathcal{F}(A_{0},u_{0})
for all large
h
and hence, by (
2.8
) and (
2.9
),

(A.1)

\sup\limits_{h\geq 1}P(A_{h})\leq a:=\frac{\mathcal{F}(A_{0},u_{0})+b_{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\Sigma)+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial\Omega)}{b_{1}}

and

\sup_{h\geq 1}\,\lambda_{h}||A_{h}|-\mathtt{v}|\leq\mathcal{F}(A_{0},u_{0})+b_{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\Sigma).

This implies
|A_{h}|\to\mathtt{v}
as
h\to\infty.
By compactness, there exists a finite perimeter set
A\subset\Omega
and a not relabelled subsequence such that
\chi_{A_{h}}\to\chi_{A}
a.e. in
\mathbb{R}^{n}.
In particular,
|A|=\mathtt{v}.

Further we assume
|A_{h}|<\mathtt{v}
for all
h;
the case
|A_{h}|>\mathtt{v}
can be treated analogously. As in the proof of
[
26
, Theorem 1.1]
given
\epsilon\in(0,2\epsilon_{n})
, where
\epsilon_{n}>0
will be chosen later, there exist small
r>0
and
x_{r}\in\Omega
such that
B_{r}(x)\subset\subset\Omega
and

|A\cap B_{r/2}(x_{r})|<\epsilon r^{n},\qquad|A\cap B_{r}(x_{r})|>\frac{\omega_{n}r^{n}}{2^{n+2}}.

For shortness, we suppose that
x_{r}=0
we write
B_{r}:=B_{r}(x_{r}).
Since
A_{h}\to A
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),
for all large
h,

|A_{h}\cap B_{r/2}|<\epsilon r^{n},\qquad|A_{h}\cap B_{r}|>\frac{\omega_{n}r^{n}}{2^{n+2}}.

Let
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the bi-Lipschitz map which takes
B_{r}
into
B_{r}
defined as

\Phi(x):=\begin{cases}(1-(2^{n}-1)\sigma)x,&|x|<\frac{r}{2},\\
x+\sigma\Big(1-\frac{r^{2}}{|x|^{2}}\Big)x,&\frac{r}{2}\leq x<r,\\
x,&|x|\geq r\end{cases}

for some
\sigma\in(0,\frac{1}{2^{n}}).

Recall from
[
26
, pp. 420-422]
that
the Jacobian
J\Phi
of
\Phi
satisfies

J\Phi(y)\geq 1+C_{1}(n)\,\sigma\qquad y\in B_{r}\setminus B_{r/2},

for some
C_{1}(n)>0,

and

J\Phi(y)\leq 1+2^{n}n\sigma\qquad y\in B_{r}.

Moreover, the tangential Jacobian
J_{n-1}T_{x}
of
\Phi
on the tangent space
T_{x}
of
\partial^{*}A_{h}
satisfies

(A.2)

J_{n-1}T_{x}\leq 1+(1+2^{n}(n-1))\sigma,\qquad x\in B_{r}\cap\partial^{*}A_{h}.

Set

(A.3)

E_{h}:=\Phi(A_{h}),\qquad v_{h}:=u_{h}\chi_{A_{h}\setminus B_{r}}+u_{0}\chi_{E_{h}\cap B_{r}}.

Note that
|E_{h}|<\mathtt{v}
and
E_{h}\Delta A_{h}\subset\overline{B_{r}}.
Let us estimate

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(A_{h},u_{h})-\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(E_{h},v_{h})=

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{B_{r}}\cap\partial^{*}A_{h}}\varphi(x,\nu_{A_{h}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-\int_{\overline{B_{r}}\cap\partial^{*}E_{h}}\varphi(x,\nu_{E_{h}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\int_{\overline{B_{r}}\cap J_{u_{h}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{u_{h}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-2\int_{\overline{B_{r}}\cap J_{v_{h}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{v_{h}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}\cap A_{h}}W(x,\mathcal{E}u_{h}-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}x-\int_{B_{r}\cap E_{h}}W(x,\mathcal{E}v_{h}-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}x

(A.4)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lambda_{h}\Big(|E_{h}|-|A_{h}|\Big):=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

By the definition of
v_{h}
and the nonnegativity of
\mathcal{W},

I_{3}\geq 0
and

I_{2}\geq-2\int_{\partial B_{r}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{v_{h}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\geq-2b_{2}n\omega_{n}r^{n-1}.

Moreover, by (
A.2
) and the area formula as well as from (
2.8
) and (
A.1
)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}\cap\partial^{*}E_{h}}\varphi(x,\nu_{E_{h}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}=

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}\cap\partial^{*}A_{h}}\varphi(\Phi(y),\nu_{A_{h}})J_{n-1}T_{y}\,d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2b_{2}(1+2^{n}(n-1)\sigma)\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(B_{r}\cap\partial^{*}A_{h})\leq 2b_{2}(1+(1+2^{n}(n-1))\sigma)a.

Moreover, by (
2.8
)

\int_{\partial B_{r}\cap\partial^{*}E_{h}}\varphi(x,\nu_{E_{h}})d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}\leq 2b_{2}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\partial B_{r})\leq 2n\omega_{n}b_{2}r^{n-1},

thus,

I_{1}\geq-2b_{2}(1+(1+2^{n}(n-1))\sigma)a-2n\omega_{n}b_{2}r^{n-1}.

Finally, repeating the same arguments of Step 4 in the proof of
[
26
, Theorem 1.1]
, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{4}\geq\lambda_{h}\sigma r^{n}\Big[C_{1}(n)\,\frac{\omega_{n}}{2^{n+1}}-C_{1}(n)\epsilon-(2^{n}-1)n\epsilon\Big],

thus,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(A_{h},u_{h})-\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(E_{h},v_{h})\geq

\displaystyle\lambda_{h}\sigma r^{n}\Big[C_{1}(n)\,\frac{\omega_{n}}{2^{n+1}}-C_{1}(n)\epsilon-(2^{n}-1)n\epsilon\Big]

(A.5)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2b_{2}(1+(1+2^{n}(n-1))\sigma)a-2n\omega_{n}b_{2}r^{n-1}.

Now if we define

\epsilon_{n}:=\frac{C_{1}(n)\omega_{n}}{2^{n+2}[1+C_{1}(n)+(2^{n}-1)n]},

then from (
A.5
) applied with
\epsilon=\epsilon_{n}
we deduce

\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(A_{h},u_{h})-\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(E_{h},v_{h})\geq\lambda_{h}\sigma\epsilon_{n}r^{n}-C

for some
C
independent of
h.
Thus,
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(A_{h},u_{h})>\mathcal{F}^{\lambda_{h}}(E_{h},v_{h})
for all sufficiently large
h,
which contradicts to the minimality of
(A_{h},u_{h}).

∎

Remark A.2
.

The same proof of Proposition
A.1
works also with
\mathcal{F}_{p}
and
\mathcal{F}_{\rm Dir}
in Theorems
2.8
and
2.9
. Indeed, in case
\mathcal{F}_{p},
for configuration
(E_{h},v_{h}),
given by (
A.3
), the equality (
A.4
) is written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\lambda_{h}}(A_{h},u_{h})-\mathcal{F}_{p}^{\lambda_{h}}(E_{h},v_{h})=

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{B_{r}}\cap\partial^{*}A_{h}}\varphi(x,\nu_{A_{h}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-\int_{\overline{B_{r}}\cap\partial^{*}E_{h}}\varphi(x,\nu_{E_{h}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\int_{\overline{B_{r}}\cap J_{u_{h}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{u_{h}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}-2\int_{\overline{B_{r}}\cap J_{v_{h}}}\varphi(x,\nu_{J_{v_{h}}})\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{n-1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{B_{r}\cap A_{h}}W_{p}(x,\mathcal{E}u_{h}-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}x-\int_{B_{r}\cap E_{h}}W_{p}(x,\mathcal{E}v_{h}-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lambda_{h}\Big(|E_{h}|-|A_{h}|\Big):=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

The estimates of
I_{1},I_{2}
and
I_{4}
are the same, and by (
2.15
) for
I_{3}
we have

I_{3}\geq\int_{A_{h}\cap B_{r}}W_{p}(x,\mathcal{E}u_{h}-\mathbf{M}_{0})\mathrm{d}x\geq-\int_{B_{r}}|f|\mathrm{d}x,

which is independent of
h.

Similarly, in case of
\mathcal{F}_{\rm Dir}
we define
v_{h}
in (
A.3
) as

v_{h}=u_{h}\chi_{A_{h}\setminus B_{r}}

and the proof runs as in the case of
\mathcal{F}_{p}.

A.2.
Some properties of GSBD-functions

Lemma A.3
.

Let
U
be an open set and
A\subset BV(U;\{0,1\})
. Assume that
u,v\in GSBD^{2}(U).
Then
u\chi_{A}+v\chi_{U\setminus A}\in GSBD^{2}(U).

Proof.

Recall that by
[
18
, Remark 9.3]
if Since
w\in GSBD^{2}(U),
then the Radon measure

\mu_{w}(B):=\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(B\cap J_{w})+\int_{B}|\mathcal{E}w|\mathrm{d}x\quad\text{ for all Borel sets $B\subset U,$}

can be used in
[
18
, Definition 4.1]
.
Thus,
u\chi_{A}+v\chi_{U\setminus A}
belongs to GSBD since, as
A
has finite perimeter in
U,
the measure

\lambda(B)=\mu_{u}(A\cap B)+\mu_{v}(B\setminus A)+\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(B\cap\partial^{*}A)\quad\text{for all Borel sets $B\subset U$}

can be used in Definition 4.1 of
[
18
]
. Since

\mathcal{E}u\chi_{A}+v\chi_{U\setminus A}=\mathcal{E}u\chi_{A}+\mathcal{E}v\chi_{U\setminus A},
it follows that
u\chi_{A}+v\chi_{U\setminus A}\in GSBD^{2}(U).

∎

Note that this property does not hold for
GSBV
-functions, because the condition
u\chi_{A}+v\chi_{U\setminus A}\in GSBV(U)
requires some regularity of the traces of
u
and
v
along
U\cap\partial^{*}A.
From Lemma
A.3
we get

GSBD^{2}({\rm Int}{(\Omega\cup S\cup\Sigma)})=GSBD^{2}(\Omega\cup S).

Lemma A.4
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a connected bounded Lipschitz open set and let
u\in GSBD^{2}(D)
be such that
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(J_{u})=0.
Then
u\in H^{1}(D)
and there exists a rigid displacement
a
such that

\|u-a\|_{H^{1}(D)}\leq C_{n,D}\|\mathcal{E}u\|_{L^{2}(D)}

for some constant
C_{n,D}>0
depending only on
n
and
D.

Proof.

Recall that by the Poincaré-Korn inequality for any connected Lipschitz set
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
there exists
C_{n,U}>0
such that

(A.6)

\|v-a\|_{H^{1}(U)}\leq C_{n,U}\|\mathcal{E}v\|_{L^{2}(U)}

for any
v\in H^{1}(U)
and for some rigid displacement
a:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}.
Obviously,
C_{n,U}
is independent of translation, and let us show

(A.7)

C_{n,\lambda U}\leq C_{n,U}\quad\text{for any $\lambda\in(0,1].$}

We may assume
0\in U.
Note that (
A.6
) is equivalent to

(A.8)

\min\limits_{\text{$a$ rigid}}\|v-a\|_{H^{1}(U)}\leq C_{n,U}\|\mathcal{E}v\|_{L^{2}(U)},\quad v\in H^{1}(U).

Fix any
u\in H^{1}(\lambda U)
and let
v_{\lambda}(x):=u(\lambda x).
Then
v_{\lambda}\in H^{1}(U),

\int_{U}|v_{\lambda}(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x=\lambda^{-n}\int_{\lambda U}|u(y)|^{2}\mathrm{d}y

and

\int_{U}|\nabla v_{\lambda}(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x=\lambda^{2-n}\int_{\lambda U}|\nabla u(y)|^{2}\mathrm{d}y,\quad\int_{U}|\mathcal{E}v_{\lambda}(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x=\lambda^{2-n}\int_{\lambda U}|\mathcal{E}u(y)|^{2}\mathrm{d}y.

Then for any rigid displacement
a(x)=\mathbf{M}x+b
we have

\displaystyle\|u-a\|_{H^{1}(\lambda U)}^{2}=

\displaystyle\lambda^{n}\|v_{\lambda}-a_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(U)}^{2}+\lambda^{n-2}\|\nabla v_{\lambda}-\mathbf{M}\|_{L^{2}(U)}^{2}\leq\lambda^{n-2}\|v_{\lambda}-a_{\lambda}\|_{H^{1}(U)}^{2},

where
a_{\lambda}(x)=\lambda\mathbf{M}x+b.
Now taking
a_{\lambda},
satisfying (
A.6
) with
v=v_{\lambda},
we have

\displaystyle\|u-a\|_{H^{1}(\lambda U)}^{2}\leq\lambda^{n-2}\|v_{\lambda}-a_{\lambda}\|_{H^{1}(U)}^{2}\leq C_{n,U}^{2}\lambda^{n-2}\|\mathcal{E}v_{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(U)}^{2}=C_{n,U}^{2}\|\mathcal{E}u\|_{L^{2}(\lambda U)}^{2},

and thus, from (
A.8
) we get (
A.7
).

Now we prove the lemma. By
[
37
, Proposition A.3]

u\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)
and hence, by (
A.6
) we just need to show
u\in H^{1}(D).

Step 1.
First assume additionally that
D
is simply connected and
0
is in the interior of
D.

Consider the sequnce

D_{i}=(1-2^{-i})D,\quad i\in\mathbb{N},

of rescalings of
D.
Since
D_{i}\subset\subset D
and
u\in H^{1}(D_{i})
by (
A.6
) and (
A.7
) there exists a rigid displacement
a_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

(A.9)

\|u-a_{i}\|_{H^{1}(D_{i})}\leq C_{n,D}\|\mathcal{E}u\|_{L^{2}(D_{i})}.

Consider the sequence
\{a_{i}\}.
Since
D_{1}\subset D_{i}\subset D,
by (
A.9
)

\|a_{i}-a_{1}\|_{H^{1}(D_{1})}\leq\|u-a_{i}\|_{H^{1}(D_{i})}+\|u-a_{1}\|_{H^{1}(D_{1})}\leq C_{n,D}\|\mathcal{E}u\|_{L^{2}(D)}.

Thus,
\{a_{i}\}
is uniformly bounded in
H^{1}(D_{1}).
Since
a_{i}
are linear, up to a subsequence,
a_{i}\to a_{0}
in
H_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
a_{i}\to a_{0}
a.e. in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some rigid displacement
a_{0}.
Hence, by (
A.9
)

\displaystyle\|u-a_{0}\|_{H^{1}(D_{i})}=\lim\limits_{j\to+\infty}\|u-a_{j}\|_{H^{1}(D_{i})}\leq\limsup\limits_{j\to+\infty}\|u-a_{j}\|_{H^{1}(D_{j})}\leq

\displaystyle C_{n,D}\,\limsup\limits_{j\to+\infty}\|\mathcal{E}u\|_{L^{2}(D_{j})}

Since
D_{j}\nearrow D
and
\mathcal{E}u\in L^{2}(D),
by the monotone convergence theorem

\|u-a_{0}\|_{H^{1}(D_{i})}\leq C_{n,D}\|\mathcal{E}u\|_{L^{2}(D)}.

Letting
i\to+\infty
in this inequality and using again the monotone convergence theorem we get

u-a_{0}\in H^{1}(D),
and thus,
u\in H^{1}(D).

Step 2.
Now consider the general case. Since
D
is Lipschitz, for any
x\in\partial D
there exists a cylinder
R_{x}
such that
D\cap R_{x}
is a subgraph of a Lipchitz function. In particular,
D\cap R_{x}
is Lipschitz and simply connected. For
x\in D
let
R_{x}
be largest cube centered at
x
and contained in
D.
Then

\overline{D}\subseteq\bigcup_{x}R_{x}
and hence, by the compactness of
\overline{D}
, there exists finitely many points
x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}
such that
\overline{D}\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}R_{x_{j}}.
Since
R_{x_{j}}\cap D
is simply connected, by Step 1,
u\in H^{1}(R_{x_{j}}\cap D)
and there exists a rigid displacement
a_{j}
such that

\|u-a_{j}\|_{H^{1}(R_{x_{j}}\cap D)}\leq C_{n,R_{x_{j}}\cap D}\|\mathcal{E}u\|_{L^{2}(R_{x_{j}}\cap D)}.

Thus,

\|u\|_{H^{1}(D)}^{2}\leq\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\|u\|_{H^{1}(D\cap R_{x_{j}})}^{2}\leq 2\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\|u-a_{j}\|_{H^{1}(D\cap R_{x_{j}})}^{2}+2\sum\limits_{j=1}^{m}\|a_{j}\|_{H^{1}(D\cap R_{x_{j}})}^{2}<+\infty.

∎

Data availability

The manuscript has no associated data.
